L432: The clinical development program of berotralstat allowed enrollment of HAE patients with moderate or mild renal impairment in the clinical trials. Using CL_{CR} to estimate renal function, 25 of 223 subjects (11.2%) enrolled across the two long-term prophylactic studies (204 and 302) who received berotralstat 150 mg QD had mild (60 to &lt;90 mL/min CL_{CR}, n=23) or moderate (30 to &lt;60 mL/min CL_{CR}, n=2) renal impairment at baseline. In these 25 subjects, the safety profile of berotralstat 150 mg QD was generally similar to that of the overall study population or those with normal renal function in both studies.
L433: Overall, considering the above factors, the increased C_{max} in subjects with severe renal impairment is not expected to cause clinically meaningful QTc prolongation, and no dose modification is recommended for patients with severe renal impairment. The PK of berotralstat in patients with ESRD requiring hemodialysis has not been determined.
L434: ## Covariates (Age, Body Weight)
L435: Based on population PK analyses of pediatric patients 12 to &lt;18 years of age (simulated bodyweight generally 40 to 71 kg), slightly higher exposure (~15%) of berotralstat was predicted compared to adults weighing 60 to 80 kg. The higher exposure in this lower bodyweight group is not expected to cause meaningful QT prolongation, which is a major safety concern correlated with exposure. Therefore, no dose modification is recommended for adolescents and adults weighing 40 to 60 kg. See the pharmacometrics review (Section 16.3.2) by Jing Niu for further details.
L436: ## **Are There Clinically Relevant Food–Drug Or Drug–Drug Interactions, and What is the Appropriate Management Strategy?**
L437: ## Effect of Food
L438: The effect of food on berotralstat exposure was evaluated in two phase 1 studies. Study BCX7353-101 was a pilot study in which six subjects received two single doses of berotralstat 218 mg (250 mg [SN]), in the fasted state and after a high-fat meal. In this study, the API in HPMC capsules (an earlier formulation) was used. Subsequently, a second relative bioavailability and food-effect study (103; three-period, crossover) evaluated berotralstat
L439: (300 mg) administered as the API in the HPMC capsule used in early phase 1 studies and the (b) (4)-capsule used in the phase 3 study. Twenty-four subjects were randomized to the following treatments:
L440: - Treatment A: Reference API in capsule, fasted
- Treatment B: Test phase 3 (b) (4)-capsule, fasted
- Treatment C: Test phase 3 (b) (4)-capsule, fed
L441: The relative effect of a high-fat meal on the PK of berotralstat is shown in Table 18. In the pilot food effect Study 101, berotralstat exposure (AUC) under fed conditions was increased 1.4-fold; however, the 90% CIs around the point estimates were large, likely as a result of the small number of subjects. In Study 103, although the median T_{max} was delayed about 3 hours, food did not impact the rate or extent of berotralstat exposure. As berotralstat progressed into the phase 3 program, the Applicant decided to administer berotralstat with food because there was no effect of food on PK and it was postulated that food may minimize GI AEs of berotralstat. Considering this, it is recommended that berotralstat be administered with food in patients with HAE.
L442: Later in clinical development, a change in the phase 3 formulation was made and a higher strength capsule (e.g., one 110/150 mg capsule instead of two 55/75 mg capsules) was developed as the to-be-marketed (TBM) drug product. The phase 3 formulation and TBM formulation are of identical composition (except for the difference in capsule fill weight) and were bridged in Study 113. The TBM drug product was bioequivalent to the phase 3 formulation for the 110 and 150 mg doses in terms of the PK parameters of interest: AUC_{0-72}, AUC_{0-inf}, and C_{max}. Although a definitive food effect study for the commercial formulation of berotralstat was not conducted, the food effect results from Study 103 are considered applicable to the TBM drug product because the phase 3 clinical study formulation is of composition identical to the TBM formulation and Study 103 evaluated the food effect at a dose of 300 mg.

L447: ## Drug Interactions
L448: ## *Effects of Coadministered Drugs on the Pharmacokinetics of Berotralstat*
L449: ## 1. Cytochrome P450-Mediated Interactions
L450: Using in vitro studies of recombinant cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes, the Applicant demonstrated that the greatest decrease in berotralstat concentration (2.7\mu\text{M}) occurred with CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 (15.0% and 22.4% decrease, respectively, with ~10% decreases in incubations without cofactors). Given the low level of in vitro metabolism, no single enzyme contributed more than 25% of berotralstat elimination; therefore, no clinical studies were conducted with strong index inhibitors of CYP3A4 and CYP2D6. Similar to the in vitro studies, the mass balance Study 104 showed the presence of multiple metabolites at low levels, none of which exceeded 8% of drug-related radioactivity.
L451: ## 2. Transporter Interaction Studies
L452: The in vitro studies showed that berotralstat was a substrate of P-gp and BCRP, but not of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, MATE1, or MATE2-K.
L453: Study 105 was conducted to assess the drug interaction potential related to transporters. The probe inhibitor, cyclosporine (inhibits P-gp, OATP, and BCRP) was used to evaluate a possible interaction with berotralstat. As shown in Table 19, cyclosporine increased the exposure of a single dose of berotralstat (C_{\text{max}} by 25% and area under the curve from time zero to infinity by 69%). The Applicant proposed no dose adjustment with concomitant administration of P-gp and BCRP inhibitors. However, the following issues were noted by the reviewer:
L454: - Although a higher dose of 300 mg QD was evaluated in phase 2 Study 203 and concluded to be generally safe, the study duration was relatively short (28 days), and the number of subjects exposed to 300 mg QD was small (N=18), reducing the level of support for higher exposure of berotralstat.
- Limited information from long-term studies is available on the safety of berotralstat with P-gp and BCRP inhibitors. According to the information submitted by the Applicant, 22 of 223 subjects (9.9%) enrolled across the two long-term prophylactic studies (204 and 302) took concomitant P-gp inhibitors while receiving berotralstat 150 mg QD. In seven subjects, the P-gp inhibitor was a short-term antibiotic (azithromycin, clarithromycin) or antifungal (fluconazole). Thirteen subjects initiated a concomitant P-gp inhibitor (atorvastatin, simvastatin, telmisartan, carvedilol, or diltiazem) administered chronically with berotralstat 150 mg QD. While no AEs attributable to concomitant dosing of a P-gp inhibitor were reported, the relevance of these data is unclear because the selectivity/sensitivity of these P-gp inhibitors is unknown and may not represent the worst-case scenario. Additionally, this interaction assessment was conducted post hoc. Typically, such analyses should be prespecified to avoid effects of confounding factors on detecting a specific clinically meaningful change in drug exposure.
- Note that cyclosporine inhibits P-gp as well as BCRP. Further studies using more selective inhibitors were not conducted by the Applicant to elucidate the relative contribution of each transporter.
L455: ## NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094
## ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L456: Overall, dose adjustment of berotralstat to 110 mg QD with concomitant administration of P-gp and/or BCRP inhibitors is recommended to be included in the prescribing information.
L457: P-gp inducers (e.g., rifampin, St. John's wort) may decrease berotralstat plasma concentration, leading to reduced efficacy of berotralstat. The Applicant's clinical development program did not address the potential impact of P-gp inducers on the systemic exposure of berotralstat. The use of berotralstat with P-gp inducers is not recommended.
L458: ## *Effect of Berotralstat on the Pharmacokinetics of Coadministered Drugs*
L459: In vitro, berotralstat inhibited CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 with IC50 values of 0.24 and 0.36\muM, respectively. The IC50 values were 1 to 10\muM for CYP2D6 (2.3\muM) and CYP3A (2.5 and 2.2\muM). All other CYP isozymes had IC50 values &gt;10\muM, which exceeded the therapeutic Cmax for berotralstat, approximately 0.28\muM (158 ng/mL) following administration of 150 mg berotralstat QD. In a time-dependent inhibition assay, IC50 shifts of 5 to 12\muM suggested berotralstat to likely be a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP3A.
L460: Berotralstat inhibited P-gp, BCRP, MATE1, and MATE2-K with calculated IC50s of 0.492, 12.0, 3.53, and 4.60\muM, respectively. The IC50s for berotralstat on OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2 could not be assessed at concentrations up to 8.9\muM. Berotralstat was an inhibitor of OATP1B1, OCT2, and OATP1A2 with IC50s of 13.2, 13.3, and 11.3\muM, respectively. Berotralstat at concentrations up to 30\muM resulted in &lt;50% inhibition of OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, and OATP2B1. Berotralstat at concentrations up to 100\muM resulted in &lt;50% inhibition of MRP2.
L461: Based on these results, in Study 105 berotralstat was tested as a transporter inhibitor using the probe substrates digoxin (P-gp substrate) and rosuvastatin (BCRP substrate).
L462: The clinical drug interaction potential for berotralstat was evaluated in the following studies:
L463: - Study 112: Phase 1 drug-drug interaction (DDI) study to evaluate the effect of berotralstat on the PK of danazol (CYP3A4), amlodipine (CYP3A4), and desipramine (CYP2D6) in healthy subjects.
- Study 115: Phase 1, single sequence, open-label, DDI study to evaluate the effect of berotralstat at steady-state on CYP3A4 (midazolam), 2C9 (tolbutamide), 2C19 (omeprazole), and 2D6 (dextromethorphan) activity using cocktail probe substrates.
- Study 105: Phase 1 study to evaluate the effect of berotralstat on the single-dose PK of a P-gp substrate (digoxin) and BCRP substrate (rosuvastatin).
L464: The results of these studies are presented in Table 19. Note that a second cocktail drug interaction study (102) was conducted using the 300 mg dose of berotralstat. This study was subsequently repeated with the 150 mg dose; only the results for 150 mg berotralstat are listed in Table 19.

L469: ## NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094
## ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L470: Berotralstat increased the exposure of digoxin by ~50% (Table 19). The increase in exposure of digoxin is likely clinically meaningful, given its narrow therapeutic range. Therefore, therapeutic drug monitoring and dose titration of digoxin is recommended if administered concomitantly with berotralstat.
L471: CYP2D6 is a polymorphic enzyme, and variations of the gene give rise to alleles resulting in no, decreased, normal, or increased enzymatic activity. In Studies 102, 112, and 115, subjects with no and increased enzymatic activity, known as poor and ultrarapid metabolizers, respectively, were excluded, but subjects with normal and decreased enzymatic activity; i.e., normal and intermediate metabolizers, respectively, were eligible for enrollment. The increase in exposure of the CYP2D6 substrates, desipramine and dextromethorphan, caused by coadministration with berotralstat is not expected to cause variability in exposure to CYP2D6 substrates greater than the inherent intersubject variability between poor metabolizers, who have no CYP2D6 enzymatic activity, and normal or ultrarapid metabolizers.
L472: Berotralstat is a moderate inhibitor of CYP3A4 and CYP2D6 sensitive substrates; therefore, appropriate monitoring and dose titration is recommended in cases of concomitant administration of sensitive substrates or those with a narrow therapeutic index that are predominantly metabolized by CYP2D6 and CYP3A4.
L473: ## What is the Bioanalytical Method Utilized to Quantitate Berotralstat?
L474: Berotralstat (BCX7353) plasma levels were determined by a validated liquid chromatography tandem-mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) assay. The method employs a least-squares linear regression analysis with a 1/x^2 weighting factor to generate a 1 to 1000 ng/mL range. Protein precipitation is performed on a 50 µL aliquot of plasma using acidified acetonitrile. The samples are next centrifuged and the supernatants are injected into a high-performance LC-MS/MS system. Plasma concentrations are back-calculated from the berotralstat peak area ratios compared with those of the calibration standards. The intra-assay precision of the method ranged from 2.9% to 7.3% (8.6% at the lower limit of quantification [LLOQ]), and intra-assay accuracy (bias) ranged from 88.9% to 110.7% (83.5% at LLOQ). Interassay precision ranged from 4.0% to 8.5% (12.1% at LLOQ), and interassay accuracy from 92.0% to 102.7% (96.1% at LLOQ). Berotralstat in human plasma was found to be stable after four freeze/thaw cycles (-20^{\circ}\text{C} and -80^{\circ}\text{C}) and for 363 days when stored frozen at -80^{\circ}\text{C}. This validated plasma assay (vendor, (b) (4)) was used to support analyses of berotralstat in Studies 101, 102, 103, 104, 105, 107, 108, 109, and 203.
L475: The Applicant utilized an additional vendor (b) (4) to quantitate berotralstat levels during the clinical development program. The (b) (4)-validated plasma bioanalytical method (171354 (b) (4)) by LC-MS/MS was used to support analysis of berotralstat in Studies 106, 112, 113, 115, 204, and 302. The method was similar to that used by (b) (4) and a cross-validation (TD-VAL-20-7353-001R) of the two assays was performed. The intra-assay precision of the plasma method ranged from 3.0% to 4.4% (4.5% at LLOQ), and intra-assay accuracy (bias) from 99.9% to 105% (104% at LLOQ). Interassay precision ranged from 3.5% to 5.3% (4.9% at LLOQ) and interassay accuracy from 103.5% to 107.3% (106% at LLOQ). For
L476: NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094 ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L477: processing purposes, BCX7353 was stable in whole blood for 2 hours at room temperature and in an ice bath. BCX7353 in human plasma was found to be stable on the bench top for at least 26 hours, after five freeze/thaw cycles (-20^{\circ}and -80^{\circ}C), and for 43 days when stored frozen at -20^{\circ}C or -80^{\circ}C, the longest period tested.
L478: The performance of the bioanalytical assays used in the clinical pharmacology studies is shown in &lt;u&gt;Table 20&lt;/u&gt;. The incurred sample reanalysis met the acceptance criteria. Samples were analyzed within the established freezer stability limit at -80^{\circ}C. In all bioanalytical reports, the interassay accuracy and precision of quality control samples and calibration standards were within the acceptance criteria: accuracy within \pm 15\% of the nominal concentration (\pm 20\% at the LLOQ) and precision of \leq 15\% (\leq 20\% at the LLOQ).

L492: ## 7.2. Review Strategy
L493: Support for the efficacy and safety of berotralstat in the proposed indication is primarily based on a single, 24-week multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group trial in 120 adult and adolescent patients with type I or II HAE, Study BCX7353-302. The efficacy results from Study BCX7353-302 are presented in Section 8.1.1. The FDA biostatistician, Mingyu Xi, Ph.D. confirmed the Applicant's efficacy analyses and generated tables and figures for this review. For the evaluation of safety, FDA medical officer Shaz Siddiqi, M.D. analyzed data from Study BCX7353-302 Parts 1 and 2, as well as from the open-label extension (OLE) of Phase 2 study BCX7353-204 using CoreFit Data Analysis, JMP, JMP Clinical, JReview, MAED, Office of Computational Science (OCS) Analysis Studio, and the Demographic Tool in the OCS Analysis Toolbox. The safety results in Section 8.3 are from the analysis by the medical officer reviewer. For brevity, trials/studies may also be referred to in this review by the three-digit study number 302 or 204.
L494: The intent-to-treat (ITT) population included all randomized subjects, irrespective of whether they received the study drug. This was the primary population used for the efficacy analyses as well as for reporting of demographic information and baseline disease characteristics. Data were analyzed according to randomized treatment.
L495: The safety population included all subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. This population was used in the assessment and reporting of safety data.
L496: ## 8. Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
L497: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L498: ## 8.1.1. Study BCX7353-302 Protocol
L499: ## Trial Design
L500: Study BCX7353-302 (Figure 5) is a phase 3, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group, three-part study in adolescent and adult subjects 12 years of age and older with type I or II HAE. The schedule of assessments is shown in Table 22. The trial was multinational; most centers were in the United States with some in Europe and Canada. The primary objective of Part 1 of the study was to determine the safety and efficacy of prophylactic berotralstat 110 or 150 mg administered orally once daily for 24 weeks compared to placebo in subjects with HAE. The primary efficacy analysis (the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the entire 24-week treatment period) was performed after the last subject completed Part 1. The primary objective of parts 2 and 3 of the study was to assess long term safety with chronic use of berotralstat.
L501: To be eligible for randomization for Part 1, subjects had to experience two HAE attacks meeting the requirements outlined in the inclusion criteria. These two attacks needed to occur in the 14
L502: ## NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094
## ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L503: to 56 day-long window starting at the date of the screening visit. The baseline attack rate included all qualifying attacks during the run-in period and up to the date of first dose.4 Parts 1, 2, and 3 were conducted in sequence, with Parts 2 and 3 conducted as continuous rollovers from Parts 1 and 2, respectively.
L504: ## Part 1 (24-Week Blinded Evaluation of the Efficacy and Safety of Berotralstat)
L505: The population of the single pivotal study comprised adolescent (12 to 17 years of age) and adult subjects (\ge 18 years of age). Eligible subjects were equally randomized to one of the following three treatment groups:
L506: - Group 1: Berotralstat 110 mg administered orally once daily for 24 weeks
- Group 2: Berotralstat 150 mg administered orally once daily for 24 weeks
- Group 3: Placebo administered orally once daily for 24 weeks
L507: Randomization into the treatment groups was stratified by the baseline HAE attack rate (\ge 2 versus &lt;2 attacks/month). Study visits in Part 1 occurred at screening, baseline, and weeks 2, 4, 8, 12, 18, and 24. The primary efficacy analysis occurred after the last subject completed their week-24 visit and included all data through week 24.
L508: ## Part 2 (24-Week Blinded Evaluation of the Safety of Berotralstat)
L509: Part 2 of the study began with administration of the study drug at the week-24 visit. Subjects in Groups 1 and 2 continued to receive the same berotralstat dose to which they were randomized in Part 1 of the study in a blinded manner. Subjects originally randomized to placebo in Group 3 underwent a second randomization to receive either 110 or 150 mg (1:1) in a blinded manner beginning at the week-24 visit through week 48. Study visits during Part 2 occurred during weeks 26, 28, 32, 36 and 48, with telephone contact at weeks 40 and 44. Efficacy and safety assessments continued throughout Part 2, including investigator confirmation of HAE. Interim safety analyses were conducted while Part 2 was ongoing.
L510: ## Part 3 (48-Week Evaluation of the Safety of Open-Label Berotralstat)
L511: Part 3 of the study began with the administration of the study drug at the week-48 visit and is ongoing. Subjects continued to receive the same berotralstat dose in Part 3 as in Part 2, albeit in an open-label manner. Study visits during Part 3 were scheduled to occur during weeks 60, 72, 84, and 96, with telephone contact at weeks 52, 56, 64, 68, 76, 80, 88, and 92. A final study follow-up visit was scheduled approximately 3 weeks following the last administration of the study drug (week 99). Investigator confirmation of attacks was not required in Part 3.
L512: 4 While the run-in period was 14-56 days, the period over which the baseline attack rate was calculated included the entire time between screening and start of study drug (i.e., run-in period + time between run-in period and 1st dose of study drug).

L520: Abbreviations: AE = adverse event; AE-QoL = Angioedema Quality of Life; ALP = alkaline phosphatase; ALT = alanine aminotransferase; AST = aspartate aminotransferase; BMI = body mass index; C1-INH = complement 1 esterase inhibitor; C3 = complement 3; C4 = complement 4; CK-MB = creatine kinase MB isoenzyme; ECG = electrocardiogram; eCRF = electronic case report form; e-diary = electronic diary; EQ-5D-5L = EuroQoL 5-dimensional, 5-level questionnaire; FSH = follicle stimulating hormone; GGT = gamma-glutamyl transpeptidase; HAE = hereditary angioedema; HBV = hepatitis B virus; HCV = hepatitis C virus; HIV = human immunodeficiency virus; HLA = human leukocyte antigen; IXRS = interactive (web or voice) response system; LFT = liver function test; LLN = lower limit of normal; NGAL = neutrophil gelatinase-associated lipocalin; PD = pharmacodynamic; PK = pharmacokinetic; QTcF = QT interval corrected by Fridericia's formula; TSQM = Treatment Satisfaction Questionnaire for Medication; WPAI = Work Productivity and Activity Impairment Questionnaire
L521: ## Trial Population
L522: The study population comprised adults (18 years or older) and adolescents (12 to 17 years of age) with a confirmed diagnosis of type I or II HAE and a minimum baseline attack rate of two investigator-confirmed attacks during the run-in period of 14 to 56 days from the screening visit.
L523: ## Key Inclusion Criteria
L524: - Males and nonpregnant, nonlactating females ≥12 years of age.
- Bodyweight of ≥40 kg.
- A confirmed diagnosis of type I or II HAE based on:
L525: A clinical diagnosis of HAE Type I or Type II, defined as having a C1-INH functional level below 50% and a complement 4 (C4) level below the lower limit of the normal (LLN) reference range, as assessed during the Screening period. In the absence of a low C4 value drawn during the intercritical period (ie, subject is not having an HAE attack), one of the following was acceptable to confirm the diagnosis of HAE: a SERPING-1 gene mutation known or likely to be associated with HAE type I or II assessed during the screening period; a confirmed family history of C1-INH deficiency; a C4 redrawn and retested during an attack in the screening period with the results below the LLN reference range. For subjects with C1-INH function ≥50% but less than the assay LLN, a SERPING-1 gene mutation known or likely to be associated with HAE type I or II, as assessed during the screening period OR a repeat C1-INH functional level &lt;50% will be considered acceptable for enrollment.
L526: 4. The subject must have had at least two HAE attacks that met all of the requirements below during the run-in period of a maximum of 56 days from the screening visit:

- The attacks were unique, defined as an attack that began at least 48 hours after the end of a previous attack.
- The attacks must have been treated, required medical attention, or been documented to cause functional impairment based on information in the e-diary.
- The attacks included symptoms of swelling. Symptoms of swelling, in addition to visible swelling, may include symptoms in the oropharyngeal or abdominal region indicative of internal swelling.
- The attacks were confirmed by the investigator to be HAE attacks. Subjects were contacted within 2 business days of the attack to discuss the attack and any queries on the data entered in the e-diary, as applicable.
L527: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L528: - Use of C1-INH for prophylaxis of HAE attacks within the 14 days prior to the screening visit or initiation during the study. Use of a C1-INH for treatment of acute attacks was not excluded at any time, nor was C1-INH for preprocedural prophylaxis for an unplanned/unforeseen procedure.
- Use of androgens or tranexamic acid for prophylaxis of HAE attacks within the 28 days prior to the screening visit or initiation during the study.
- Use of an angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitor within 7 days of the baseline visit or planned initiation during the study.
- Any significant cardiovascular disease or sudden cardiac death.
- Laboratory abnormalities: Creatinine clearance \le 30 mL/min and aspartate aminotransferase (AST) or ALT level greater than three-fold the ULN.
- Prior enrollment in a berotralstat study.
- Pregnant, planning to become pregnant during the study, or nursing.
L529: ## Key Subject Discontinuation Criteria
L530: A subject was discontinued from the study drug for any of the following reasons, which was recorded in the source documents and the electronic case report form:
L531: - Emergence of any laboratory abnormality or AE that in the judgment of the investigator compromised the ability of the subject to continue study-specific procedures or altered the subject's benefit-risk profile such that it was considered not to be in the subject's best interest to continue.
- Confirmed ALT or AST elevation as follows: greater than eight-fold the ULN; greater than five-fold the ULN for &gt;2 weeks; greater than three-fold the ULN and a total bilirubin level greater than two-fold the ULN; and greater than three-fold the ULN if accompanied by fatigue, nausea, vomiting, right upper quadrant pain and tenderness, fever, rash and/or eosinophilia (&gt;5\%).
- Subject noncompliance with the study drug or the study protocol.
- A QTcF of &gt;500 ms (confirmed on repeat ECG testing).
L532: 5. Subjects with a study drug-related grade 3 or 4 rash as described by the Division of Microbiology and Infectious Diseases (DMID) skin-mucocutaneous criteria were discontinued from the study and treated according to best medical practice.
L533: In all cases, subjects who discontinued the study drug during Part 1 were asked to complete all regularly scheduled visits and procedures through week 24.
L534: *Reviewer comments: The Applicant's target population and overall study design was consistent with HAE programs for other approved products for prophylaxis. A similar patient population, primary endpoint, and treatment period to assess efficacy (26 weeks) were used in the recent lanadelumab program.*
L535: *Although HAE is a rare disease, the development program for HAE prophylaxis is established and the primary clinical endpoint of HAE attacks is a meaningful, established endpoint. The randomized, controlled treatment period of 26 weeks is acceptable for this program. Most sites in the pivotal trial were located in the United States; therefore, the data should be reflective of the U.S. population of patients with HAE.*
L536: *The inclusion, exclusion, and subject discontinuation criteria were also acceptable and consistent with prior studies of approved HAE products. Long-term prophylaxis of HAE is an individualized decision made between the patient and physician taking into account disease activity, frequency of attacks, quality of life, health-care resources, and ability to achieve adequate control with on-demand therapy. However, based on the minimum attack frequency, the eligibility criteria captured a target population likely to require prophylactic therapy.*
L537: ## Study Treatments and Blinding
L538: - Berotralstat 110 mg orally once daily
- Berotralstat 150 mg orally once daily
- Placebo orally once daily
L539: The study drug was blinded to both the subjects and study staff. With the exception of the week-2 and week-26 visits, subjects were instructed to bring all drug kits (including unused and used bottles) and e-diaries with them to each study visit. Compliance and adherence were reviewed at these visits. During study drug administration, subjects were to record in the e-diary the times of day the study drug was taken on a daily basis. Real-time access to e-diary entries for subjects was available via a website, and the investigator (or designee) proactively assessed compliance.
L540: ## Concomitant Medications
L541: ## Allowed
L542: Therapies for coexisting conditions, treatment for acute HAE attacks (including C1-INH for acute-attack therapy, but not for long-term prophylaxis), treatments for short-term HAE prophylaxis, and therapies for any AEs.
L543: ## Prohibited
L544: Long-term prophylaxis for HAE (e.g., C1-INH, androgens, antifibrinolytics), angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors, estrogen-containing medications with systemic absorption, androgens, and any other investigational drug or device.
L545: ## Study Endpoints
L546: ## Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L547: The primary endpoint for Part 1 is the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the entire 24-week treatment period, defined as day 1 to the time of the first dose of study drug in Part 2 (day 1 to day 168).
L548: Details of acute attacks of angioedema were recorded in an electronic diary (e-diary) beginning at screening and continuing through week 48. Subjects were required to complete the HAE attack e-diary daily, noting whether or not symptoms of an HAE attack were experienced in the previous 24 hours. Subjects were required to complete the e-diary irrespective of the presence of HAE symptoms. If a subject did report an attack, additional details about the attack were required to be entered into the e-diary, including the start and stop times, symptoms, anatomical location of swelling (if applicable), severity, treatment(s) administered and the times of administration, and whether additional medical care was sought for the attack. Attack severity was assessed by the subject as negligible, mild, moderate, or severe. Attacks were treated in accordance with the subject's normal standard of care. Medications for angioedema attacks were not provided by the Applicant.
L549: For all attacks that occurred from screening through the end of Part 1, the investigator (or designee) reviewed the e-diary record of the attack. Subjects were contacted within approximately 2 business days of the end of the attack to discuss its clinical characteristics, any questions the investigator had on the entered data or to gain additional information not included in the e-diary that the investigator deemed important for clinical evaluation of the event. This information, in conjunction with the e-diary record, was used by the investigator to verify or reject the event recorded in the e-diary as an HAE attack. Investigators used their judgment to confirm or reject a reported event as an HAE attack. All investigator-confirmed attacks of HAE must have included symptoms of swelling irrespective of treatment. In addition to visible swelling, symptoms in the oropharyngeal or abdominal regions indicative of internal swelling were also included. Investigator-confirmed attacks were entered into the AE database as mild, moderate, or severe. Safety and tolerability were evaluated by assessments of AEs, laboratory parameters (clinical chemistry, hematology, and urinalysis), vital signs, ECGs, and physical examinations.
L550: ## Secondary Efficacy Endpoint
L551: Three secondary efficacy endpoints were specified in the Statistical Analysis Plan:
L552: - Change from baseline in the Angioedema Quality of Life Questionnaire (AE-QoL) total score at week 24.
- Number and proportion of days with angioedema symptoms through 24 weeks.
L553: - Rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the effective treatment period, defined as beginning on day 8 through to the first dose of study drug in Part 2.
L554: Quality-of-life data were obtained using the Angioedema Quality of Life Questionnaire (AE-QoL) and Treatment Satisfaction Questionnaire for Medications (TSQM)5 at baseline and subsequently at weeks 4, 8, 12, 18, and 24 (Table 22).
L555: The AE-QoL is an AE-specific, quality-of-life questionnaire used in the clinical setting and captured in some prior HAE clinical trials. The AE-QoL consists of 17 questions with five answers each and has a recall period of 4 weeks. A total score can be computed over all questions, as in Study 302 Part 1, but it is also possible to apply the AE-QoL as a profile instrument by grouping the questions into four domains (*Functioning*, *Fatigue/Mood*, *Fears/Shame*, and *Nutrition*) and calculating the individual domain scores. These are transformed to a linear 0 to 100 scale, with higher scores indicative of greater QoL impairment (0 best, 100 worst). Therefore, based on the scoring algorithm a decrease in score should correspond to improvement in QoL (Section 16.4). The 14-item TSQM is used in clinical practice to assess patients' satisfaction with medication. It comprises scores on four scales: side effects, effectiveness, convenience, and global satisfaction.
L556: ## Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints
L557: Three exploratory efficacy endpoints were specified in the Statistical Analysis Plan:
L558: - Number and proportion of subjects with no attacks over 24 weeks.
- Use of HAE attack medications over 24 weeks.
- The proportion of responders to study drug, defined as at least a 50% relative reduction in the rate of investigator-confirmed attacks.
L559: *Reviewer comments: A reduction in the frequency of acute HAE attacks is a clinically meaningful outcome for HAE patients and therefore acceptable as a primary endpoint for an HAE clinical trial intended to demonstrate efficacy for a product indicated for prophylactic treatment. Investigator-confirmed attacks (rather than patient-reported attacks) have been used as the primary efficacy endpoint in other HAE programs to ensure that only swelling attacks and not prodromal symptoms or pre-emptive rescue medication use are counted as a distinct HAE attack. Secondary endpoints primarily assessed outcomes related to HAE attack reduction.*
L560: *Endpoints derived from the AE-QoL instrument are not considered well-defined and reliable assessments of HAE-related quality of life for use by the clinical division in HAE prophylaxis trials or fit for purpose for labeling. While its use in recurrent angioedema patients has been described in the literature, this includes patients with recurrent histamine or bradykinin-mediated angioedema. Furthermore, the recall period is relatively long at 4 weeks, and the tool contains domains related to nutrition and mood that may not be specific to HAE. Within person*
L561: 5 Bharmal, M, K Payne, MJ Atkinson, MP Desrosiers, DE Morisky, and E Gemmen, 2009, Validation of an abbreviated Treatment Satisfaction Questionnaire for Medication (TSQM-9) among patients on antihypertensive medications, Health Qual Life Outcomes, 7:36.
L562: *clinically meaningful change has not been established for the AE-QoL or derived endpoints; therefore, the clinical meaningfulness of any results are uncertain.*
L563: ## Safety Assessments
L564: Safety was evaluated by assessments of AEs, laboratory parameters (clinical chemistry, hematology, urinalysis, coagulation, drug screen, and additional tests), vital signs, ECGs, and physical examinations according to the schedule in Table 22. Furthermore, should a pregnant woman have received blinded study drug, the outcome of the pregnancy was to be followed.
L565: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L566: ## Statistical Methods
L567: The efficacy analyses were conducted on the ITT population, defined as all randomized subjects, regardless of whether study drug was administered. The primary endpoint was the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the entire treatment period of Part 1, expressed as attacks per month (1 month was defined as 28 days). Comparisons of the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks between each berotralstat dose and placebo during the entire dosing period for Part 1 was conducted using a Poisson regression model. The number of investigator-confirmed attacks was included as the dependent variable, the treatment was included as a fixed effect, the baseline attack rate was included as a covariate, and the logarithm of duration on treatment was included as an offset variable. The estimated monthly rate of attacks for each treatment group and the treatment differences expressed as attack rate ratios (berotralstat over placebo rate ratio) and their associated 95% confidence intervals (CIs) were obtained from the Poisson regression model.
L568: ## *Analyses of Secondary Endpoints for Part 1*
L569: The AE-QoL total score change from baseline was analyzed using a mixed model for repeated measures with fixed effects for treatment, baseline attack rate, baseline AE-QoL, visit, a visit-by-treatment interaction, and a random effect for subject. The estimated treatment difference for each berotralstat dose versus placebo at each visit, the 95% CI, and the associated p-value were calculated. Primary inferences were drawn from treatment differences for the changes from baseline estimated from the mixed model for repeated measures model at week 24.
L570: The number and proportion of days with angioedema symptoms through week 24 were computed. The proportion of days with angioedema symptoms through week 24 was evaluated using an analysis of covariance model with baseline attack rate as a covariate and treatment included as a fixed effect. The estimated treatment difference comparing each active treatment to placebo, the 95% CI, and the associated p-value were calculated.
L571: For investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during the effective treatment period, between-treatment comparisons were performed using a Poisson regression model similar to that used for the primary endpoint analysis.
L572: The Statistical Analysis Plan specified that for all analyses using the Poisson regression model, goodness of fit will be evaluated by examining the dispersion parameter. In addition, the
L573: Applicant proposed to analyze the data using a negative binomial model to evaluate the dispersion parameter. If the dispersion parameter of the negative binomial model was significantly greater than zero, the negative binomial model was to be considered primary.
L574: ## Multiplicity Control
L575: A combination of hierarchical testing and the Hochberg step-up procedure was used to control the Type I error rate for testing the primary and secondary endpoints in Part 1. Four endpoints were evaluated, for each of which two potential doses were tested against placebo. The four endpoints were tested in a hierarchical fashion, and the two doses were tested using the Hochberg step-up procedure at each level of the hierarchy to which both doses progress.
L576: The first endpoint to be tested was the primary endpoint, the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the entire 24-week treatment period of Part 1. Using the Hochberg step-up procedure, each of the two doses was tested at the \alpha=0.05 level, comparing active treatment to placebo. If the maximum of the two p-values was &lt;0.05, the null hypotheses of no difference between the rate of attacks for subjects on active and placebo treatment was rejected for both doses and testing proceeded to the next endpoint in the hierarchy with \alpha=0.05. If the maximum of the two p-values was &gt;0.05 but the minimum was &lt;0.025, the null hypothesis for the dose with p&lt;0.025 was rejected and testing for that dose only proceeded to the next endpoint in the hierarchy with \alpha=0.025. Otherwise, the null hypotheses for both doses were not rejected, testing stopped, and the next endpoint in the hierarchy could not be tested.
L577: The first, second, third, and fourth endpoints in the hierarchy are:
L578: - The rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the entire 24-week treatment period (day 1 to day 168).
- Change from baseline in the total AE-QoL score at week 24.
- Number and proportion of days with angioedema symptoms through 24 weeks.
- Rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during dosing in the effective treatment period (beginning on day 8 through 24 weeks).
L579: The process described above was continued for each endpoint in the hierarchy until either all four endpoints had been tested or testing was stopped due to nonrejection of the null hypotheses for both doses for endpoints earlier in the hierarchy. At each level of the hierarchy, the Hochberg step-up procedure was used to control the Type I error rate if two doses were tested. Otherwise, if only one dose was tested, the single test was conducted with \alpha=0.025.
L580: ## Subgroup Analysis
L581: For the primary endpoint, subgroup analyses were conducted by the following categories:
L582: - Age (12 to 17, 18 to 64, and \ge 65 years)
- Gender (male and female)
- Race (white and other)
- Region (North America and Europe)
L583: ## Missing Data Sensitivity Analysis
L584: Missing data sensitivity analyses were conducted for the analysis of the primary endpoint using three methods:
L585: - A missing data sensitivity analysis was conducted in which observed data post-study drug discontinuation were included in the attack-rate determination.
- A missing data sensitivity analysis was conducted in which observed data post-study drug discontinuation were used in combination with imputed data for subjects who discontinued study drug prior to the end of Part 1 and did not continue to provide attack information.
- A tipping-point analysis was conducted in which observed data post-study drug discontinuation were combined with imputed data for subjects who discontinued study drug prior to the end of Part 1 and did not continue to provide attack information by varying assumptions for missingness.
L586: The analyses of safety data for Parts 1, 2, and 3 and of effectiveness data for Parts 2 and 3 were primarily descriptive.
L587: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L588: The Applicant amended the protocol once, making the following key changes:
L589: ## Amendment 1 (October 11, 2018)
L590: - Removed participating study centers in the Asia-Pacific region.
- Added objectives and endpoints for Study 302 Part 3.
- Clarified the diagnosis of HAE, which allowed patients with C1-INH function \ge 50\% but lower than the lower limit of normal (LLN) to be included in study.
- Provide updated information on the berotralstat clinical program.
L591: ## **8.1.2. Study BCX7353-302 Part 1 Results**
L592: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L593: The Applicant conducted clinical studies according to procedures that incorporate the ethical principles of good clinical practices (GCP). BioCryst also undertook a GCP audit program. Audits were performed by auditors from [REDACTED](b) (4) who operated independently of the study monitors. The audits within the clinical program were aimed at study documentation, investigator sites, and clinical study reports. A total of 10 site audits was performed through April 10, 2019 and audit certificates were provided. No issues were identified.
L594: ## **Compliance with GCP**
L595: The Applicant states that the study was conducted in accordance with GCP as described in the guidelines of the ICH. The study protocol, amendments, informed consent, and other necessary documents were reviewed and approved by an independent ethics committee or institutional review board before initiation of the study. Written informed consent and assent, where applicable, were obtained from each subject before participating in the study.
L596: ## Financial Disclosure
L597: The Applicant has adequately disclosed its financial interests and arrangements with clinical investigators as recommended in the guidance for industry entitled Financial Disclosure by Clinical Investigators (Financial Disclosure).
L598: ## Subject Disposition
L599: Overall, a total of 160 subjects were screened, 121 subjects were randomized (ITT population), and 120 of these subjects (99%) were treated (safety population). Most screening failures were caused by normal C1-INH and/or C4 results and an insufficient number of baseline HAE attacks during the run-in period. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was randomized to placebo but not treated due to withdrawal of consent; this subject was included in the ITT population but not the safety population. A total of 108 subjects (89%) completed study drug dosing in Part 1: 37 subjects who received berotralstat 110 mg (90%), 37 who received berotralstat 150 mg (93%), and 34 who received placebo (85%). No subject was lost to follow up. Table 23 shows the subject disposition.

L603: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L604: A total of four subjects had major protocol deviations that resulted in exclusion from the per protocol population:
L605: - Berotralstat 110 mg group:Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) received a prohibited concomitant medication (desogestrel) that may have affected the HAE attack rate.
L606: ## NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094
## ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L607: - Berotralstat 150 mg group:Subject (b) (6) was informed of the run-in qualifying attack requirement during the screening period. This subject did meet the run-in attack rate requirement, but the protocol specified that the run-in attack rate should not be disclosed to study subjects; therefore, the sponsor listed as a protocol deviation.
- Placebo group:Subject (b) (6) was randomized to placebo and was dispensed study drug prior to completion of all baseline procedures. The subject was never dosed with study drug. The subject withdrew consent and discontinued the study on May 31, 2018.
- Subject (b) (6) received a prohibited concomitant medication (prophylactic Berinert).
L608: Although these major protocol deviations were unlikely to have affected the overall quality or interpretation of the study data, two of the four patients continued in the trial despite receiving adjunct and prohibited HAE therapies that may have improved their treatment response. In view of the small total sample size, there was concern this could be significant. However, an efficacy analysis of the ITT population yielded robust results whether these two patients were included or excluded from the ITT population. Numerous other minor protocol deviations occurred; these were related to the timing of study visits, timing of procedures, and one ECG assessment that showed a PR interval longer than recommended for entry. The extended PR interval was deemed not to be clinically meaningful by the site principal investigator and cardiologist.
L609: ## Demographic Characteristics
L610: The demographic characteristics of the subjects are shown in Table 24, and were generally similar across treatment arms. Overall, the study enrolled more females than males. The proportion of females was higher in the berotralstat 110 mg group (73%) than in the berotralstat 150 mg group (57%). Relatively small proportions of the subjects were in the youngest (12 to 17 years) and oldest (\ge65 years) age groups. The majority of subjects (93% across treatment arms) were white. Most subjects (72%) were from North America with over 60% of that population from the United States. The remainder were from various European countries. Also, there was a total of six adolescent patients enrolled, which is relatively few, even for this orphan population. However, this may be expected because symptoms tend to present for the first time in puberty and patients are typically diagnosed several years after initial presentation.
L611: *Reviewer comment: Although HAE affects males and females equally, the literature reports a clear female predominance, most likely because women tend to be more symptomatic than men as a result of hormonal factors such as puberty, contraception, and pregnancy. Therefore, the predominantly female study population does not raise concerns about the generalizability of the study but is merely a reflection of the most symptomatic patients, who are most likely to use and benefit from prophylactic treatment. All ethnicities and races are affected by types I and II HAE; however, most of the extant epidemiologic and genetic data are derived from European*
L612: populations. The interaction between race and disease expression is poorly understood. The proportion of nonwhite patients in this study, while relatively small, is adequate for such a rare disease. The proportion of adolescent subjects in this study is small (N=6); however, the pathophysiology of the disease is unaffected by age.

L617: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., Disease Characteristics, Important Concomitant Drugs)
L618: The baseline disease characteristics were generally similar across treatment groups (Table 25). The vast majority of patients had HAE diagnosis confirmed by a low C4 and &lt;50% functional level of C1-inhibitor (88%). The Applicant did not provide data to categorize subjects as having
L619: type I or II HAE; however, low C4 and low functional C1-INH are pathognomonic for types I and II HAE. Type III HAE is exceedingly rare and characterized by normal complement studies. Based on prevalence data, the vast majority of subjects (~85%) are expected to have type I disease. Further, phenotypically types I and II HAE are indistinguishable and respond similarly to HAE treatments. SERPING-1 gene mutations were only evaluated if a patient had a normal C4 level and despite a clinical history suggestive of type I or II HAE. The time between screening and first dose of study drug was similar between treatment groups. The mean baseline attack rate per month was also similar among the treatment groups, with an average of approximately three attacks per month. Subjects reported the primary location of previous HAE attacks as a combination of abdominal and peripheral attacks. All of the treatment groups had on average about two laryngeal attacks per year. The study population reflects a population with predominantly moderate-to-severe HAE and a fairly equal disease burden among treatment groups in terms of the frequency of attacks and attack location; including laryngeal involvement. Patients were most likely to be on prior prophylactic therapy with androgens, followed by C1 inhibitor replacement. This was consistent in all three treatment groups. Overall, the study population was representative of a population of patients with HAE for which long-term prophylaxis with an oral kallikrein inhibitor would be appropriate.
L620: *Reviewer comment: The baseline attack rate was calculated from the start of the run-in period to the date of first dose of study drug. Once subjects experienced 2 or more attacks during the 14 to 56-day run-in period, they were randomized into the study, but may not have received the first dose of study drug right away. Some subjects had a longer time interval between the end of run-in and first dose of study drug (up to 16 days); given the variability of disease, these subjects may not have experienced any further attacks during this time period. Because the baseline attack rate was calculated from the run-in period to the first day of study drug, some subjects had a baseline attack rate &lt; 1 attack per month; this scenario was most apparent in subjects with a long run-in period and lengthier delay between run-in and start of study drug.*

L628: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L629: The rate of treatment compliance was high and fairly equal across all treatment groups, with an average total mean compliance rate of 98% (Table 26). Subjects were not allowed to take concomitant prophylactic HAE medications; however, they were allowed rescue medications for an HAE attack that occurred during the study. Rescue medications were not supplied by the Applicant.

L640: The total number of investigator-confirmed attacks was lower in the 150 mg group than the 110mg dose, and berotralstat-treated subjects had fewer total attacks than subjects treated with placebo. Numerically, the proportion of subjects with one or more HAE attacks requiring treatment with rescue medication was lowest in the 150 mg group. Notably, however, very few subjects in the study achieved complete elimination of attacks during the treatment period: one (2%), three (8%), and two (5%) subjects in the 110 mg, 150 mg, and placebo groups, respectively.
L641: Analysis of the HAE attacks during the 24-week treatment period confirmed that the primary endpoint was met for both dose levels, with the 110 and 150 mg doses reducing the HAE attack rate by 30% (p=0.024) and 44% (p&lt;0.001), respectively, relative to placebo. The negative binomial regression analysis-estimated attack rate per 28 days was 1.65 for berotralstat 110 mg
L642: subjects, 1.31 for berotralstat 150 mg subjects, and 2.35 for placebo subjects over the 24-week Part 1 dosing period. Table 28 presents the analysis results of the primary endpoint.

L648: The Applicant reported that 10 to 15% of subject-reported HAE attacks were not confirmed by the investigator either due to absence of swelling symptoms or investigator judgment. However, rejection rates were similar across treatment groups, and the Applicant's supplementary analysis of subject-reported attacks showed that the rate ratios and p-values were similar to those from the primary endpoint analysis.
L649: In an ad hoc analysis of the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks rated as moderate or severe by the subject during Part 1, the 110 and 150 mg berotralstat doses reduced the rate of moderate and severe HAE attacks by 10% (p=0.608) and 40% (p=0.018), respectively, compared with placebo.
L650: ## Dose/Dose Response
L651: Subjects receiving the 150 mg dose had a moderately higher reduction in the HAE attack rate compared to those receiving the 110 mg dose. Attack rate ratio between 150 mg and 110 mg doses is 0.80 with 95% CI (0.58, 1.10). The dose response is preserved in the evaluation of secondary and exploratory endpoints, which also demonstrate numerically better outcomes with the 150 mg dose compared to the 110 mg dose. Within the development program, there were more treatment discontinuations due to lack of perceived efficacy in the 110 mg group compared to the 150 mg group, albeit this was mainly seen in the open label studies. Overall, the evidence supports the Applicant's recommended dose of 150 mg once daily.
L652: ## Durability of Response
L653: Figure 6 presents the mean investigator-confirmed attack rate by month. Berotralstat reduced the attack rate in month 1 and the effect was stable over the 6-month duration of Part 1.

L657: ## Persistence of Effect
L658: Patients in this study continued the study drug in an open-label fashion in Part 3. Because the effect of berotralstat was not evaluated after cessation of study drug, no data are available to analyze the persistence of its effect.
L659: ## Efficacy Results—Secondary or Exploratory Clinical Outcome Assessment (Patient-Reported Outcome) Endpoints
L660: ## Change from Baseline in AE-QoL Total Score at Week 24
L661: **Table 29** shows the analysis of the secondary endpoint of AE-QoL score at week 24. The least-squares mean (LSM) differences from placebo in AE-QoL total score were -2.8 (95% CI -10.1, 4.5; p=0.453) and -4.9 (95% CI -12.2, 2.4; p=0.188) for the berotralstat 110 and 150 mg groups, respectively. The treatment effect of berotralstat compared to placebo was not statistically significant for either dose group. Given that the AE-QoL total score did not significantly improve with berotralstat treatment, the individual components and domains were not analyzed. Because this endpoint failed to reach statistical significance and the Applicant did not seek a labeling claim for AE-QoL, the Division of Clinical Outcome Assessments was not consulted to evaluate this tool during the NDA review.

L680: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trials
L681: ## Exploratory Efficacy Analysis
L682: One of the exploratory efficacy endpoints was the proportion of responders to study drug, defined as at least a 50% relative reduction in the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during treatment compared with the baseline attack rate. The analysis of this endpoint is presented in Table 32. For this exploratory endpoint, 58% of patients receiving 150 mg berotralstat and 51% of those receiving 110 mg berotralstat had a \ge 50\% reduction in their HAE attack rate compared to baseline versus 25% of patients receiving placebo.
L683: The post hoc analyses of the proportion of responders to study drug, defined as at least 70% and 90% relative reductions in the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during treatment compared with the baseline attack rate, were also considered. The analyses of these endpoints are also listed in Table 32.
L684: In post hoc analyses, 50% and 23% of patients receiving 150 mg berotralstat, and 27% and 10% of those receiving 110 mg berotralstat, had a \ge 70\% or \ge 90\% reduction in HAE attack rate compared to baseline versus 15% and 8% of patients receiving placebo, respectively. While berotralstat demonstrated significant improvements in investigator-confirmed attack rates compared to placebo, these responses suggest that very few subjects experienced complete/near complete elimination of attacks. These responder analysis results should be interpreted with caution because the reduction of attack rate relative to the baseline rate could be potentially highly variable due to the limiteseline observation period.

L689: ## Subgroup Analysis
L690: To examine whether the treatment effects vary by baseline demographic factors, such as age, gender, race, or region, we conducted subgroup analyses on the primary endpoint for these four factors. These subgroup analyses used the same analysis model used in the overall analyses.
L691: **Figure 7** presents the subgroup analysis results of primary endpoint investigator-confirmed HAE attack rate comparing the 110 mg group to the placebo group. **Figure 8** presents the subgroup analysis results comparing the 150 mg group to the placebo group. Subgroup analyses showed that the treatment effect was consistent with the overall effect across all categories, with the exception of 3 subgroups whose sample sizes were too small to allow adequate interpretation (adolescents [n=6], elderly [n=9], and other race [n=8]). Due to the small number of subjects in those subgroups, model based analysis could not be completed or the results were not reliable. We left fields of these subgroups blank on the graphs and only reported the number of subjects in each group. Because HAE is caused by genetic mutations leading to low levels of functional C1-INH, the pathophysiology of the disease is the same irrespective of age. Even though the number of adolescents evaluated in the pivotal trial was small, efficacy in adolescents is expected to be similar to that in adults and may be extrapolated from the adult data.
L692: Figure 7. Subgroup Analysis of the Investigator-Confirmed HAE Attack Rate Comparing the 110 mg Group vs Placebo Group (ITT population): Study 302 Part 1

L694: Note: Due to extremely small number of subjects in subgroups of adolescents and other race, model-based estimate of rate ratio and CIs are considered unreliable and misleading. Therefore, they are not presented in the plot.

L700: ## Missing Data Sensitivity Analysis
L701: The Applicant conducted three missing data sensitivity analyses on the primary endpoint. The first missing data sensitivity analysis included observed data post-treatment discontinuation. The analysis was supportive of the primary endpoint for both dose levels, with the 110 and 150 mg doses reducing the HAE attack rate by 31% (95% CI 6, 49; p=0.020) and 42% (95% CI 20, 58; p&lt;0.001), respectively, versus placebo. The negative binomial analysis-estimated attack rate per 28 days was 1.60 for berotralstat 110 mg subjects, 1.34 for berotralstat 150 mg subjects, and 2.31 for placebo subjects over the 24-week Part 1 dosing period.
L702: The second missing data sensitivity analysis included observed data post-treatment discontinuation and imputing missing data post-treatment discontinuation. The analysis was supportive of the primary endpoint for both dose levels, with the 110 and 150 mg doses reducing the HAE attack rate by 31% (95% CI 5, 50; p=0.023) and 35% (95% CI: 9, 53; p=0.011), respectively, versus placebo. The negative binomial analysis-estimated attack rate per 28 days
L703: was 1.64 for berotralstat 110 mg subjects, 1.55 for berotralstat 150 mg subjects, and 2.37 for placebo subjects over the 24-week Part 1 dosing period.
L704: The third missing data sensitivity analysis was the tipping point analysis on the primary endpoint. The analysis showed that three and two-fold increase in expected attack rates for the 110 and 150 mg doses (attack rates of 5.0 and 2.6, respectively) would be needed for the missing data to alter the result of the analysis. The expected attack rate at week 24 for the 100 and 150 mg groups was 1.65 and 1.31, respectively. Therefore, it is highly unlikely that the conclusion would be altered by the missing data.
L705: Overall, the missing data sensitivity analyses of both doses of berotralstat were supportive of the robustness of the outcomes of the primary analysis.
L706: ## 8.2. Statistical Issues
L707: ## 8.2.1. Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L708: The effectiveness of berotralstat was studied in a single ongoing Study 302 based on its Part 1 results. The study met its primary endpoint; the 110 and 150 mg doses of berotralstat reducing the rate of HAE attacks by 30% (p=0.024) and 44% (p&lt;0.001) relative to placebo, respectively.
L709: The effectiveness of berotralstat was also evaluated in terms of the secondary endpoints of change from baseline in the AE-QoL, number and proportion of days with angioedema symptoms, and rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks during the effective treatment period. Testing of the first secondary endpoint, a patient-reported outcome in the hierarchy, failed for both doses, halting formal efficacy evaluation on the remaining secondary endpoints. Of note, in an exploratory analysis, the trend favoring berotralstat in the proportion of patients with at least a 50% reduction in HAE attacks compared to baseline lends support to the primary findings.
L710: The study achieved clinical and statistical significance on the primary endpoint. Endpoints derived from similar data (e.g., investigator confirmed attack rate during the effective treatment period, proportion of days with angioedema symptoms in Part 1) also achieved nominal clinical and statistical significance. There were no statistically significant secondary endpoints after adjusting for multiple testing using the Hochberg procedure. Considering the rarity of the disease, the current unmet need and the robustness of the primary endpoint analysis, the statistical reviewer concludes that substantial evidence of effectiveness was met.
L711: ## 8.2.2. Multiplicity Control
L712: The study hypothesis testing combined hierarchical testing and the Hochberg step-up procedure. Testing of primary and secondary endpoints proceeded in hierarchical fashion, and two doses at each level were tested using the Hochberg step-up procedure. Table 33 summarizes the results of the hierarchical testing. Note that there were no statistically significant secondary endpoints after adjusting for multiple testing.

L717: ## 8.2.3. Estimand
L718: The primary analyses were conducted on the ITT population but included only observed on-treatment data. This is an on-treatment estimand according to the ICH E9 R1 guidance. The Applicant also conducted an analysis of all collected data, including the data collected after treatment discontinuation. The results based on this treatment-policy estimand were consistent with those based on the on-treatment estimand.
L719: ## 8.2.4. Missing Data and Sensitivity Analysis
L720: Table 34 presents the number of subjects who completed study treatment in Part 1. One subject in the 110 mg group, two subjects in the 150 mg group, and four subjects in the placebo group discontinued the study treatment early. Of them, one in the 110 mg group, one in the 150 mg group, and two in the placebo group were due to lack of efficacy.

L725: The Applicant conducted a missing data sensitivity analysis using three approaches. The first approach used all collected data including the data after treatment discontinuation but without imputation, the second approach used the same data as in the first approach plus imputation for the remaining missing data, and the third approach used all collected data plus imputed data based on the tipping point analysis method. All three analyses supported the conclusion from the primary analysis.
L726: The sensitivity analyses of both doses of berotralstat were supportive of the robustness of the primary analysis outcomes.
L727: Overall, the statistical reviewer of this NDA concludes that the efficacy of both doses of berotralstat was demonstrated. The statistical reviewer's recommended regulatory action for this application is approval.
L728: ## 8.3. Review of Safety
L729: ## 8.3.1. Safety Review Approach
L730: All clinical studies conducted as part of the berotralstat development program were evaluated for safety; however, given the short exposure periods and absence of additional safety findings in the phase 1 and shorter 2 studies, this safety review focuses on Part 1 of the 24-week placebo-controlled phase 3 trial, Study 302. Supportive data for safety were derived from the analysis of interim data from Part 2 of Studies 302 and 204, discussed in Section 8.3.4, Additional Safety Assessments and Section 8.3.7, Special Safety Studies, respectively. The review tools used to conduct independent reviewer analyses were MAED, JMP Clinical, JMP, JReview, OCS Analysis Studio, and the Demographic Tool in the OCS Analysis Toolbox.
L731: ## 8.3.2. Review of the Safety Database
L732: ## Overall Exposure
L733: The population of subjects exposed to berotralstat in Study 302 and Study 204 for type I or II HAE is outlined in Table 35. The table includes exposure from Part 2 and Part 3 of Study 302

L735: (data cutoff 12/1/19). Overall, 218 subjects have been exposed to berotralstat 110 and/or 150 mg for ≥48 weeks (337 days), and 63 subjects have been exposed for ≥72 weeks. At the time of NDA submission, 103 subjects had received 150 mg berotralstat for ≥48 weeks—73 subjects from Study 204 and 30 subjects from Study 302. With the 120-day safety update, 123 subjects had been exposed to the 150 mg dose for at least 1 year.

L740: ## Adequacy of the Safety Database
L741: A total of 15 clinical studies have been completed in the berotralstat HAE clinical development program and 6 studies are ongoing. As of April 22, 2020, a cumulative total of 1104 subjects have been enrolled in berotralstat studies. Among them, 1030 subjects received berotralstat and 165 subjects received placebo, with 91 placebo subjects also receiving berotralstat in crossover studies.
L742: Study 302 included 120 adult and adolescent patients with HAE, 81 of whom received at least one dose of berotralstat during the controlled treatment period. A total of 227 subjects received open-label berotralstat in the OLE Study 204. A total of 16 adolescent patients in the two trials were exposed to the study drug.
L743: Overall, the safety database is of sufficient size and duration for a rare disease such as HAE to assess the safety of the proposed dose of berotralstat when taken chronically. Of note, the safety database exceeds the Division's recommendation for 1 year exposure in at least 100 subjects. Study 302 Part 3 and the OLE Study 204 are ongoing to provide further long-term safety data.
L744: ## 8.3.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L745: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L746: Overall, no data quality issues were identified in the review of this NDA. OSI audits of sites 33 and 35 from Study 302 did not reveal any issues related to data integrity. As noted earlier in this review, our initial review of the ADaM datasets for pivotal Study 302 Part 1, showed that the record sequence numbers for the ADVS and ADLB datasets were missing. An IR was sent on February 12, 2020 to resubmit the ADVS and ADLB datasets for Study 302 Part 1, Study 204, the NDA analysis, and the ISS with the appropriate sequence number identifiers included. A data analysis was performed on these new datasets, which were received on February 20, 2020. Since the record sequence numbers did not change the numerical and non-numerical data, the analysis was identical to the safety reports generated previously, and did not impact the integrity of the data.
L747: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L748: The definitions of AEs and SAEs in the protocols were consistent with 21 CFR 312.32. Reports of all AEs and SAEs, regardless of investigator attribution, were collected from the time of signing of informed consent to the last study visit. Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were defined as any AE that increased in severity or that was newly developed at or after the first dose of study drug through the final follow-up visit. AEs were coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities version 19.1.
L749: All AEs were assessed (graded) for severity and classified using the DMID criteria for grading AEs (published November 2007). Any AEs not covered by the DMID criteria were assessed and classified into one of four clearly defined categories as follows: mild (1), moderate (2), severe (3), and life-threatening (4). The Applicant's coding of verbatim terms to preferred terms (PTs) was appropriate. The investigator or medically qualified designee also reviewed each AE to make a determination of its relationship to the study drug using the following categories: not related, unlikely, possibly related, probably related, and definitely related.
L750: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L751: Laboratory tests were performed at time points specified in the schedule of assessments for Studies 302. Only clinically significant abnormalities (as determined by the investigator) in laboratory values, physical examination findings, or vital signs were reported as AEs.
L752: ## 8.3.4. Safety
L753: Controlled study drug exposure is shown in Table 36. Drug exposure was fairly uniform across all three treatment groups, with an average mean exposure of 159 days for all subjects. The total exposure period for Part 1 of Study 302 was 168 days (24 weeks). There was adequate drug exposure during the study to determine the treatment effect and perform a safety analysis.

L763: ## Deaths
L764: No deaths were reported in Study 302 Part 1.
L765: ## Serious Adverse Events
L766: In Study 302, there were four patients with SAEs (Table 38). Three of these occurred in the placebo group (pneumonia, leiomyoma, and transient ischemic attack/diverticular intestinal hemorrhage in the same patient). One patient in the berotralstat 110 mg group developed myeloma. A retrospective review revealed an elevated serum protein level in this subject at initial intake prior to receiving berotralstat; therefore, myeloma was suspected to have been present prior to study drug administration. This SAE led to treatment discontinuation. There were no SAEs in the 150 mg berotralstat group, which is the Applicant's recommended dose for patients without hepatic impairment. The total number of SAEs was small, even for this
L767: relatively small safety population (N=120). In addition, most SAEs occurred in the placebo group, and no SAE was attributed to berotralstat.

L778: ## Berotralstat 150 mg
L779: Subject (b) (6) developed a grade 3 ALT elevation (238 U/L) on day 14 which progressed to grade 4 elevation (430 U/L) on day 22. The subject stopped study drug on day 17 but continued in the study. He remained asymptomatic and without other laboratory abnormalities. His ALT level normalized by day 57 (30 U/L). However, on Study Day 127, he developed a grade 2 ALT elevation which persisted as a Grade 1 elevation at the end of the study on day 169 (grade 1). He had a history of androgen use from 2013 to 2017, but a year since the most recent exposure. He also had normal liver enzyme levels at baseline.
L780: *Reviewer comment: In view of the of the subject's liver enzyme re-elevation despite earlier cessation of study drug and normalization of his ALT level, he likely had another hepatic disorder contributing to his abnormal liver enzymes. This may or may not have been accentuated by berotralstat. He did not have any reported history of alcohol or illicit drug use. He was taking cetirizine, azelastine, and fluticasone for nasal allergies.*
L781: ## Berotralstat 110 mg
L782: Subject (b) (6) discontinued study drug after 3 months of therapy due to dyspepsia, nausea and vomiting of grade 2 severity. Symptoms began on day 5 of treatment, but resolved 3 days after stopping study drug. He was also taking hydrocodone, esomeprazole and calcium as the time.
L783: *Reviewer comment: Although patient was on medication which may cause gastrointestinal distress (hydrocodone) and taking anti-reflux medication, the timing of symptom onset soon after drug initiation and prompt resolution after drug cessation, make it likely symptoms were due to berotralstat.*
L784: Subject (b) (6) discontinued study after developing a purpuric rash of grade 3 severity which was suspected by the investigator to be related to study drug. The rash began on day 78 of treatment originally affecting both arms, resolved without treatment in 14 days, then recurred 3 more times with involvement to both legs. Berotralstat was stopped on day 138; however, the rash was still present as of day 188. A skin biopsy did not show any evidence of leukocytoclastic vasculitis. She had no evidence of coagulopathy on labwork and was not taking any medications which would promote easy bruising.
L785: *Reviewer comment: Upon further review of detailed analysis of photographs provided by the Applicant, the clinical reviewer assessment is that the purpuric rash is unrelated to treatment. This was based on the morphology and distribution of the rash, its waxing and waning without steroid treatment, and persistence of the rash despite stopping the study drug 3 months prior.*
L786: Subject (b) (6) was diagnosed with grade 4 multiple myeloma on day 80. This event was categorized as an SAE and led to treatment discontinuation. It was noted that laboratory results included persistently elevated serum protein levels prior to study drug exposure and preceding the onset of the event: 10.0 g/dL at study screening, 9.7 g/dL at study baseline, 10.1 g/dL at study week 4), and 10.1 at study week 8 (normal range: 6.0 to 8.0 g/dL). Consequently, a serum
L787: protein electrophoresis test revealed gammopathy, and the diagnosis of plasma cell myeloma was confirmed by the hematology-oncology consultant.
L788: Reviewer comment: In view of the elevated serum protein at baseline, the clinical reviewer agrees with the Applicant's assessment that myeloma was likely already present in early stages at start of study entry and therefore un-related to berotralstat exposure.
L789: ## Significant Adverse Events
L790: Ten subjects reported 11 TEAEs of grade 3 or 4 severity (Table 40), several of which were discussed under SAEs or TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation. Nearly half of these events occurred in the placebo group, and only one event (back pain) occurred in the 150 mg group. Other than abdominal pain, all severe AEs were single occurrences. No new safety signals were identified based on the analysis of severe TEAEs.

L794: ## Common Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L795: The most common TEAEs occurring in ≥ 10% subjects and greater than placebo are shown in Table 41. Gastrointestinal adverse events were the most commonly reported TEAE in all treatment groups. The most common GI-related preferred terms (PTs) observed were nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain and diarrhea. Similar/related PTs were pooled according to the FDA MedDRA Query for abdominal pain (abdominal pain, abdominal discomfort, abdominal pain upper, abdominal tenderness). Abdominal distention was not included because this may signify
L796: a different disease process. GI adverse events appeared to be dose-related. Subjects in the 150 mg treatment group had more GI-related adverse events (1.5- to 2-fold higher) than the 110 mg treatment group or placebo. There was high rate of nausea across all three treatment arms with the highest frequency placebo (14.8% versus 17.9%). Other adverse reactions with an incidence of 5% to &lt;10% and a higher incidence in berotralstat-treated patients compared to those on placebo were headache (9% versus 5%), fatigue (6% versus 3%), and flatulence (6% versus 3%).

L808: ## Laboratory Findings
L809: Routine clinical testing included hematology, serum chemistry with LFTs, and urinalysis. The results of LFTs are discussed in detail below. Otherwise, there was no significant clinical difference between the berotralstat and placebo groups in laboratory parameters, including coagulation tests, during the study.
L810: ## Liver Function Tests
L811: Table 43, Table 44, and Table 45 list the maximum ALT, AST and bilirubin elevations, respectively, experienced by subjects within each treatment arm. Of note, patients with AST/ALT &gt;3× ULN were excluded from the study; therefore, most subjects had normal transaminase and bilirubin values at baseline. Few subjects experienced transaminase or bilirubin elevation; however, one subject in the 150 mg berotralstat group had marked increase in ALT that prompted discontinuation of study drug as described earlier in this review. A single subject had a maximum AST elevation &gt;3× ULN, and no subjects had bilirubin levels &gt;2× ULN. There were no potential Hy's law cases in this study or the development program as a whole.

L828: Abbreviations: BCX, berotralstat; BILI, bilirubin; ULN, upper limit of normal
L829: The Applicant asserted that a history of androgen use was a risk factor for elevated liver enzyme levels in the phase 2 and 3 studies. Table 46 shows maximum ALT elevation by prior androgen use (yes/no). Although in the 110 mg berotralstat group, the rate of ALT elevation in patients who had and had not used androgens was 2.4% and 4.9%, respectively, no such effect was seen in the 150 mg group. Patients in the placebo group with prior androgen use also tended to have a higher incidence of an elevated ALT level compared to those who had never taken androgens. Given the small sample size, it is unclear if the combination of berotralstat and prior androgen use had an additive effect or if the risk of increased ALT was present with prior androgen use alone. This potential relationship was evaluated further in safety assessments from Study 204 (Section 8.3.7.1, Table 57).
