L1184: Reviewer's comments: In general, the Applicant's population PK analysis is considered acceptable to support the labeling claims in Section 12.3 of the prescribing information. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1185: The SAEM method was used by the Applicant for parameter estimation. Other estimation methods were evaluated by the reviewer, but the model crashed using the first-order conditional estimation and first-order conditional estimation I methods. This might explain why the SAEM method was selected by the Applicant.
L1186: The reviewer noted that bilirubin and food effect were identified as significant covariates on CL and absorption parameters (Ka and bioavailability for dose in compartment 1) by statistical testing; however, both were removed before model finalization as not clinically significant. Assessment of clinical significance should be conducted after model development, and a covariate's effect on berotralstat exposure should be interpreted in the context of the known exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety. Although the reviewer does not fully agree with the Applicant's covariate analysis, this does not change the conclusion of no clinically relevant covariates and no recommended dose adjustment for covariates.
L1187: Bodyweight is a major factor that influences the exposure of berotralstat. In Table 68, the median bodyweight in the pooled dataset is 75.1 kg, within the 60 to 80 kg bodyweight group (low-weight adults) in Table 66. Higher exposure (~15%) was predicted in adults of 40 to 60 kg and adolescents 12 to 18 years of age compared to adults of 60 to 80 kg. The higher exposure in lighter patients is not expected to cause meaningful QT prolongation, which is a major safety concern; therefore, no dose adjustment is recommended. Furthermore, berotralstat was effective in subjects of high bodyweight and showed no substantial safety signal in subjects with low bodyweight (Section 16.3.2), supporting the recommendation of no dose adjustment.
L1188: ## 16.3.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1189: ## 16.3.2.1. Exposure-Efficacy Relationship
L1190: ## Applicant's Analysis
L1191: ## PK-Pharmacodynamics Relationship
L1192: Exposure-response analyses of the relationship between kallikrein inhibition and berotralstat plasma concentrations were conducted using data from subjects with HAE in Study
L1193: BCX7353-203. The effect of berotralstat on the target enzyme, plasma kallikrein, was evaluated by comparing specific amidolytic activity in plasma samples taken after drug administration to that in baseline samples. Exposure-response analyses demonstrated a high correlation between ex vivo kallikrein inhibition activity and the plasma berotralstat concentration, which was well-described by a sigmoidal maximum-efficacy model (coefficient of determination 0.75) with a calculated half-maximal effective concentration of 11 ng/mL (Figure 15). The trough concentrations of the 110 and 150 mg doses were 45 and 70 ng/mL; therefore, both reached the plateau for kallikrein inhibition.
L1194: **Figure 15. Correlation of Plasma Berotralstat Concentrations and PD Effect on Plasma Kallikrein: PK/PD Population in Study BCX7353-203**
L1195: Source: Study BCX7353-203 clinical study report, Figure 14.2.4.2
Exposure-response relationship was determined with a pharmacologically sigmoid E_{max} model in which: Kallikrein inhibition (E) = (E_{max} \times [BCX7353]y) \div (EC_{50}y + [BCX7353]y)
Circles represent individual kallikrein inhibition values at a given concentration; line represents model-predicted kallikrein inhibition across the concentration range.
Because of dilution during sample preparation for the ex vivo assay, berotralstat concentration in the ex vivo assay represents one quarter of the corresponding in vivo plasma concentration.
Model estimation: E_{max} 90.4% (CV 2.07%); EC_{50} 11.0 ng/mL (CV 4.36%); \gamma 2.08 (CV 9.49%); R 0.867; EC_{80} 44.0 ng/mL (calculated based on EC_{80} = 4 \times EC_{50}).
The model did not account for multiple data pairs within each subject; thus, the results need to be interpreted with caution.
Abbreviations: BCX7353, berotralstat; CV, coefficient of variation; EC_{50}, half-maximal effective concentration; EC_{80}, 80% effective concentration; E_{max}, maximum efficacy; PD, pharmacodynamics; PK, pharmacokinetics
L1196: ## PK–Efficacy Relationship
L1197: The efficacy endpoint was the rate of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks. In the dose-ranging Study BCX7353-203, berotralstat at doses of 55, 110, 218, and 300 mg once daily orally for 28 days were tested. The correlation between the investigator-confirmed weekly attack rate and berotralstat plasma exposure was evaluated graphically. None of the berotralstat steady-
L1198: state PK parameters (C_{max}, area under the curve over the dosing interval [AUC_{tau}], and maximum plasma concentration over the dosing interval [C_{tau}]) were correlated with the confirmed attack rate, as shown in Figure 16 for AUC_{tau}. The plots for C_{max} and C_{tau} were similar to that for AUC_{tau} and showed no correlation with the attack rate.
L1199: **Figure 16. Investigator-Confirmed Attack Rate vs Berotralstat Plasma AUC_{tau} Over the Dose Range of 55 to 300 mg With 95% CI for Mean Predicted Values: Study BCX7353-203**
L1200: Source: Study BCX7353-203 clinical study report, Figure 7
L1201: Treatments: 300, 218, 110, 55 mg
L1202: Regression equation: Confirmed attack rate (attacks per week) 0.57 + -0.00002 \times AUC_{tau}
L1203: Abbreviations: AUC_{tau}, area under the curve over the dosing interval; CI, confidence interval
L1204: The absence of a correlation might be driven by doses of \ge 110 mg because the 110, 218 and 300 mg doses showed efficacy; berotralstat significantly reduced the confirmed HAE attack rate in the 28-day treatment period versus placebo. further exposure-response analyses were conducted with the two lower doses (i.e., 55 and 110 mg). The steady-state berotralstat AUC_{tau} was correlated with the confirmed attack rate in the dose range 55 to 110 mg (Figure 17). Therefore, 110 and 150 mg free-base doses were selected for the registration trial (Study BCX7353-302).
L1205: **Figure 17. Investigator-Confirmed Attack Rate vs Berotralstat Plasma AUC_{\tau} Over the Dose Range of 55 to 110 mg With 95% CI for Mean Predicted Values in Study BCX7353-203**
L1206: Source: Study BCX7353-203 clinical study report, Figure 8
L1207: Treatments, 110 and 55 mg
L1208: Regression equation: Confirmed attack rate (attacks per week) = 0.89 - 0.00035 \times AUC_{\tau}
Abbreviations: AUC_{\tau}, area under the curve over the dosing interval; CI, confidence interval
L1209: Because bodyweight is a significant covariate that influences the exposure of berotralstat, the potential impact of bodyweight on efficacy was examined by summarizing the investigator-confirmed attack rates by bodyweight category using the data collected in Part 1 of Study 302. The mean attack rate varied among the three bodyweight categories: 1.793, 1.810, and 2.219 for berotralstat 110 mg; 1.799, 2.022, and 1.251 for berotralstat 150 mg; and 2.484, 2.015, and 2.901 for placebo low-, middle-, and high-weight subjects, respectively (Table 69). The 110 and 150 mg doses demonstrated effectiveness in high-weight subjects. The variability in efficacy among bodyweight groups may be attributable to the small sample size of each bodyweight tertile, and/or the nonstratification of baseline disease severity by bodyweight tertile.

L1214: ## 16.3.2.2. Exposure–Safety Relationship
L1215: ## Exposure–Electrocardiogram Interval Relationship (QT-Integrated Review Team Analysis)
L1216: Based on the analyses by the QT-IRT, berotralstat caused a dose- and concentration-dependent increase in the QT interval at the doses (150 and 450 mg) evaluated. However, the QT interval was not prolonged to any clinically relevant extent at the peak concentrations for the recommended dose of 150 mg once daily, and ~1.5-fold higher exposure than the recommended 150 mg dose. This covers the exposure increase (~15%) predicted for subjects of lower bodyweight (underweight adults or adolescents in Table 68). As a result, the higher exposure in lighter patients is not expected to cause meaningful QT prolongation, and no dose adjustment is recommended. Refer to the QT-Integrated Review Team review (Reference ID, 4592746) for details.
L1217: ## Applicant's Analysis
L1218: An analysis of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) by dose level was conducted using the data collected in Part 1 of Study BCX7353-302. No substantial safety findings were identified in the 110 and 150 mg groups. The TEAE analysis identified only a slightly higher frequency of gastrointestinal (GI)-related TEAEs for the higher doses: 150 mg (50.0%) &gt;110 mg (41.5%) &gt;placebo (35.9%). No clear dose-dependent trend was identified for other TEAEs.
L1219: The potential effect of bodyweight on safety was evaluated by summarizing TEAEs by bodyweight category using the data collected in the integrated phase 2/3 prophylactic long-term studies (Studies 204 and 302). Overall, there were no differences in the percentage of subjects who experienced TEAEs based on baseline bodyweight tertile: no bodyweight-based
L1220: ## NDA/BLA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 214094
## ORLADEYO (berotralstat)
L1221: trends in the frequency of drug-related TEAEs, grade 3 or 4 TEAEs, TEAEs leading to discontinuation, serious adverse events, or GI-abdominal TEAEs (Table 70). Consistent with the findings of the overall dose-response analysis for safety, lower bodyweight tended to be associated with a slightly higher frequency of GI-related TEAEs. Overall, no substantial safety signal was identified in subjects of low bodyweight.

L1248: ## LABELING RECOMMENDATION
L1249: This review recommends the following text for nonclinical sections of the ORLADEYO (berotralstat) capsule label. The nonclinical sections include the following: USE in SPECIFIC POPULATION (8.1 and 8.2), CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY (12.1), and NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY (13.1).
L1250: ## 8.1 Pregnancy
L1251: ## *Risk Summary*
L1252: There are insufficient data (b) (4) in pregnant women to inform drug-related risks with ORLADEYO use in pregnancy. Based on animal reproduction studies, no evidence of structural alterations was observed when berotralstat was administered orally to pregnant rats and rabbits during organogenesis at doses up to approximately 10 and 2 times, respectively, the maximum recommended human daily dose (MRHDD) in adults on an AUC basis [see *Data*].
L1253: The background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated population is unknown. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2-4% and 15-20%, respectively.
L1254: ## *Data*
L1255: ## *Animal Data*
L1256: In animal reproduction studies, oral administration of berotralstat to pregnant rats and rabbits during the period of organogenesis did not cause fetal structural alterations. The berotralstat dose in rats and rabbits was up to approximately 10 and 2 times, respectively, the MRHDD in adults (on an AUC basis at maternal doses of 75 and 100 mg/kg/day, respectively). In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, oral administration of berotralstat to pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis until delivery at doses up to 45 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times of the MRHD on a mg/m2 basis) did not cause fetal structural alterations. Berotralstat concentrations in the fetal blood was approximately 5 – 11% of the maternal blood.
L1257: ## 8.2 Lactation
L1258: ## *Risk Summary*
L1259: There are no data on the presence of berotralstat in human milk, its effects on the breastfed infant, or its effects on milk production. However, when a drug is present in animal milk, it is likely that the drug will be present in human milk. Low levels of berotralstat were detected in the plasma of rat pups when dams were dosed with the drug orally during the lactation period. The berotralstat concentration in the pup plasma was approximately 2% of the maternal plasma (see *Data*).
L1260: The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for ORLADEYO and any potential adverse
L1261: effects on the breastfed infant from ORLADEYO or from the underlying maternal condition.
L1262: ## Data
L1263: ## Animal Data
L1264: In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, berotralstat was administered to dams during the pregnancy and lactation periods at doses up to 45 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times of the MRHD on a mg/m2 basis). Berotralstat was detected in the plasma of pups during the lactation period. The berotralstat concentration in the pup plasma was approximately 2% of the maternal plasma. Both dams and pups at 45 mg/kg/day showed statistically significant decreases in body weight gain (p &lt; 0.05). No treatment-related effects were observed at 25 mg/kg/day (approximately equal to the MRHD on a mg/m2 basis).
L1265: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L1266: Berotralstat is a plasma kallikrein inhibitor that binds plasma kallikrein and inhibits its proteolytic activity. Plasma kallikrein is a protease that cleaves high-molecular-weight-kininogen (HMWK) to generate cleaved HMWK (cHMWK) and bradykinin, a potent vasodilator that increases vascular permeability resulting in swelling and pain associated with HAE. In patients with HAE due to C1-inhibitor (C1-INH) deficiency or dysfunction, normal regulation of plasma kallikrein activity is not present, which leads to uncontrolled increases in plasma kallikrein activity and results in angioedema attacks. Berotralstat decreases plasma kallikrein activity to control excess bradykinin generation in patients with HAE.
L1267: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L1268: ## Carcinogenesis
L1269: Wistar rats (b) (4) 26-week study in Tg.rasH2 transgenic mice. The berotralstat doses (oral gavage) were up to 20 and 50 mg/kg/day in rats and mice, (b) (4)
L1270: **Mutagenesis** Berotralstat tested negative in the *in vitro* bacterial reverse mutation assay (Ames test), the *in vitro* (b) (4)
L1271: **Impairment of Fertility** In a fertility study in rats, berotralstat at oral doses up to 45 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times the MRHD on a mg/m2 basis) showed no effect on fertility in males or females.
L1272: ## LABELING REVIEW
L1273: Edits to the nonclinical information in the ORLADEYO (berotralstat) label proposed by the Applicant are recommended. The nonclinical information is described in the following sections: 8.1 Pregnancy, 8.2 Lactation, 12.1 Mechanism of Action, and 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility. Edits are made to ensure that the to-be-approved label reflects the Agency's most current thinking.
L1274: ## 1. INTRODUCTION
L1275: This review evaluates the nonclinical information of berotralstat in the ORLADEYO label proposed by BioCryst on December 3, 2019 and July 9, 2020 (SDNs 0001 and 0029, respectively). Specifically, the review evaluates the nonclinical information in the following subsections of the label: 8.1, 8.2, 12.1, and 13.1. The review recommends major edits, including both the text and dose ratios between animals and humans.
L1276: Berotralstat is indicated for the prevention of hereditary angioedema (HAE) in patients 12 years of age and older. The proposed clinical dosing regimen is 150-mg berotralstat once daily. The mean AUC_{0-24} at 150-mg/day berotralstat in patients is 2540 ng.h/mL.
L1277: This review consists of 8 sections. Section 1 provides background information relevant to the current review. Section 2 discusses dose ratios between animals and humans in the nonclinical sections. Section 3 discusses methods and rationale used for this review. Sections 4 - 7 evaluate subsections of the proposed labeling, rationale for the recommended editing, and line editing. Section 8 provides a summary of overall recommendations. A clean copy of the recommended text of the nonclinical sections of the ORLADEYO label is presented in the LABELING RECOMMENDATION section which is located at the beginning of the document.
L1278: ## 2. EXPOSURE MULTIPLES
L1279: This review derives exposure multiples between animals and humans on an AUC and \text{mg}/\text{m}^2/\text{day} basis. Exposure multiples based on AUCs are preferred. When AUC data is unavailable, exposure multiples are calculated on a \text{mg}/\text{m}^2/\text{day} basis [human equivalent dose (\text{Human}_{\text{HED}})]. The following exposures in humans are used to calculate the exposure multiples: AUC_{0-24} = 2540 \text{ ng.h/mL} and \text{Human}_{\text{HED}} = 92.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2/\text{day}. Both parameters are based on a maximum recommended human daily dose (MRHDD) of 150 mg. A body weight of 60-kg was used to calculate the \text{Human}_{\text{HED}} [150 \text{ mg}/\text{day}/60 \text{ kg} \times 37 \text{ (km)} = 92.5 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2/\text{day}]. Briefly, all but one exposure multiple (i.e., 45 \text{mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} dose in rats) were derived on an AUC basis (Table 1).

L1284: ## 3. METHODS OF EVALUATION
L1285: The review uses product labels of drugs approved for the same indication and similar mechanism of actions as references to maintain consistency across approved labels. These products were approved for the treatment of acute attacks or prophylaxis of HAE. The current application proposed to prevent HAE attacks from occurring. See Section 6 (Table 2).
L1286: Contents of the proposed labeling are generally acceptable, but the review finds it necessary to edit the proposed text. L1289: ## *Risk Summary*
L1290: There are insufficient data (b) (4) in pregnant women to inform drug-related risks with ORLADEYO use in pregnancy. Based on animal reproduction studies, no evidence of structural alterations was observed when berotralstat was administered orally to pregnant rats and rabbits during organogenesis at doses up to approximately 10 and 2 times, respectively, the maximum recommended human daily dose (MRHDD) in adults on an AUC basis [see Data]. (b) (4)
L1291: (b) (4)
L1292: The background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated population is unknown. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2-4% and 15-20%, respectively.
L1293: ## Data
L1294: ## *Animal Data*
L1295: In animal reproduction studies, oral administration of berotralstat to pregnant rats and rabbits during the period of organogenesis did not cause fetal structural alterations. The berotralstat dose in rats and rabbits was up to approximately 10 and 2 times, respectively, the MRHDD in adults (on an AUC basis at maternal doses of 75 and 100 mg/kg/day, respectively). In a pre- and postnatal development study in rats, oral administration of berotralstat to pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis until delivery at doses up to 45 mg/kg/day (approximately 2 times of the MRHD on a mg/m2 basis) did not cause fetal structural alterations. Berotralstat concentrations in the fetal blood (b) (4) approximately 5 – 11% of the maternal blood.

L1299: ## Risk Summary
L1300: There are no data on the presence of berotralstat in human milk, its effects on the breastfed infant, or its effects on milk production. However, when a drug is present in animal milk, it is likely that the drug will be present in human milk. Low levels of berotralstat were detected in the plasma of rat pups when dams were dosed with the drug orally during the lactation period. The berotralstat concentration in the pup plasma was approximately 2% of the maternal plasma (see Data).

## **Completed by Dr. Luqi Pei in NDA 214094 on March 24, 2020 (DARRTS 4580256)**
L1334: **Date:** July 23, 2020
**NDA No.:** 214094
**Submission No.:** SDNs 0001 and 29
**Submission date:** December 3, 2019 and July 9, 2020
**Drug Name:** Berotralstat
**Reviewer:** Luqi Pei, PhD
**Subject:** Re-evaluation of tumors in the mesenteric lymph nodes in male rats in 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of berotralstat (Study 801-161)
L1335: This document is an addendum to a nonclinical review completed by Dr. Luqi Pei on March 24, 2020 in NDA 214094 (DARRTS ID 4580256). The addendum documents the Agency's re-evaluation of tumor findings in the mesenteric lymph nodes in males in a 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of berotralstat in rats (Study 806-161). The re-evaluation was prompted by the Applicant's recent submission of an amendment to the final report of Study 806-161 (SDN 029). The final report of the study was submitted to the original NDA on December 3, 2019 (SDN 0001).
L1336: Dr. Luqi Pei of the Division of Pharmacology and Toxicology for Immunology and Inflammation (DPT-II) completed a review of the final report of Study 806-161 on March 24, 2020 (DARRTS ID 4580256). The Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (Exec CAC) evaluated the findings of the study (as reported in the original study report) on March 10, 2020. The Committee concluded that berotralstat tested positive for carcinogenicity. The conclusion was based on the dose-dependent increase in the incidence of hemangiosarcoma in the lymph nodes in male rats. See Exec CAC Meeting minutes dated March 16, 2020 (DARRTS ID 4576064).
L1337: BioCryst submitted an amendment to the final report of Study 806-161 on July 9, 2020 (NDA 214094, eCTD 029). The amendment revised neoplastic findings in the vasculature, particularly the mesenteric lymph node in male rats. Table 1 presents the tumor incidence in the mesenteric lymph nodes in male rats.
L1338: **Table 1: Tumor Findings in the Mesenteric Lymph Node in Males (Study 806-161)**
L1339: eCTD	Sub Date	Report	Tumor	Incidence (n = 60)	P valuea
(C v HD)
0 mkd	3 mkd	8 mkd	20 mkd
0001	12/3/19	Original	Hemangiosarcoma	1	2	2	9	0.005
0029	7/9/20	Revised	Hemangioma	1	0	0	5	0.081b
L1340: a. Pairwise comparison.
L1341: b. Provided by Dr. Miao Zhuang via email on July 17, 2020.
L1342: Briefly, the amendment revised both tumor type and incidence in this tissue. The tumor type changed from malignant hemangiosarcoma to benign hemangioma. The tumor incidence was decreased by approximately half. These differences resulted in a change in carcinogenic potential of berotralstat: from positive to negative, according to the current evaluation criteria. That is, the original report shows that berotralstat tested positive for carcinogenicity while the amended report showed that berotralstat tested negative.
L1343: The revision of the tumor type and incidence was the result of a pathology expert working group (EWG) assembled by BioCryst to re-evaluate neoplastic findings in the mesenteric lymph nodes. The re-evaluation was prompted by a difference in interpretation between the Agency and BioCryst regarding the relevance of the tumor findings to humans, based on data presented in the original study report.
L1344: As discussed previously, the Exec CAC concluded that berotralstat tested positive because berotralstat at 20 mg/kg/day caused a statistically significant increase in the incidence of hemangiosarcoma (Ref. DARRTS ID 4576064). The Committee also concluded that the relevance of the finding to humans is unknown. However, BioCryst has maintained their position that the findings of hemangiosarcoma in rats is a species-specific effect. The Agency's position was conveyed to BioCryst in the Filing Communication dated February 14, 2020 and was briefly discussed at the mid-cycle meeting with the Applicant.
L1345: BioCryst assembled the EWG to re-evaluate the slides with tumor findings. The EWG conveyed on April 28, and May 12, 2020 via videoconference. The group reviewed "H&amp;E stained digitized slides of selected tissue sections of proliferative vascular findings in mesenteric lymph nodes and all diagnosed hemangiomas and hemangiosarcomas from other tissues as reported". Members of the EWG included Drs. Paul W. Snyder (Chair), Jerry Hardisty (reviewing pathologist), Joshua Decker (Study Pathologist), Kenneth Schaffer, Andrew Suttie, and Samuel Cohen. The EWG issued its report on June 11, 2020 (p. 2401 – 2427 of the amended study report). Table 2 summarizes the diagnosis of pathological findings in the original report and the EWG.
L1346: The Exec CAC evaluated the findings of the EWG via email communications on July 10, 2020. Based on the new re-adjudication provided in the amended study report, the Committee concluded that berotralstat is negative for tumorigenicity in male rats in Study 806-161. See the Addendum to the March 16, 2020 Exec CAC minutes completed on July 23, 2020 (DARRTS ID 4645569).

L1372: ## Summary
L1373: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in Wistar Han [Crl:WI(Han)] rats and one in CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous (transgenic) mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of ORLADEYO (BCX7353), when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L1374: **Rat Study:** Two hundred and forty Wistar Han [Crl:WI(Han)] rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated and vehicle control groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups were 3, 8 and 20 mg/kg/day. The rats in the vehicle control group received the vehicle (deionized water).
L1375: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups in male or female rats. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and each of the treated groups in male or female rats.
L1376: ## Tumor analysis:
L1377: - For male rats, the tumor data analysis showed a statistically significant positive dose response relationship in incidences of the Hemangiosarcoma, Multicentric (P-value=0.0018&lt;0.005), Hemangiosarcoma in Lymph Node, Mesenteric (P-value=0.0001&lt;0.005) and combined tumors of Hemangiosarcoma and Hemangioma in whole body (P-value=0.0044&lt;0.005). The pairwise comparison of the Hemangiosarcoma in Lymph Node, Mesenteric (P-value=0.0049&lt;0.01) between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for male rats is significant at 0.01 level of significance. L1385: ## 1. Background
L1386: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in Wistar Han [Crl:WI(Han)] rats and one in CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous (transgenic) mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of ORLADEYO (BCX7353), when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice. Results of this review have been discussed with the reviewing pharmacologist Dr. Luqi Pei. This review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received from the sponsor on December 3, 2019 via NDA214094/0000.
L1387: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as the dose increases.
L1388: ## 2. Rat Study
L1389: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were four treated groups and one vehicle control group. Three hundred Wistar Han [Crl:WI(Han)] rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated and vehicle control groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups were 3, 8, 20 and 60/40 mg/kg/day. The rats in the vehicle control group received the vehicle (deionized water). The study for the rats was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks. In accordance with study termination criteria, all surviving male rats were sacrificed during Week 105.
L1390: Animals in 60/40 mg/kg/day group were dosed at 60 mg/kg/day from Week 1-17; from Week 18-28 animals were dosed at 40 mg/kg/day. Pharm/Tox reviewer Dr. Pei requested to drop the 60/40 mg/kg/day group due to high death rate. We do not include the 60/40 mg/kg/day group in our analysis.

L1394: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1395: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L1396: Intercurrent mortality data was analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups was conducted using a log-rank test. If this overall test was significant (p&lt;0.05) and there were more than two groups, then a follow up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to the control group using a log-rank test. Results of all pair-wise comparisons were reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance levels. All endpoints were analyzed using two-tailed tests.
L1397: Sponsor's findings: Sponsor's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of survival were 43 (71.67%), 39 (65%), 42 (70%), and 33 (55%) in vehicle control, 3 mg/kg/day, 8 mg/kg/day and 20 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in males and 35 (58.33%), 36 (60%), 37 (61.67%), and 28 (46.67%) in vehicle control, 3 mg/kg/day, 8 mg/kg/day and 20 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in females.
L1398: Morbidity and mortalities occurred in the male and females at 60 mg/kg/day, causing the dose level to be lowered to 40 mg/kg/day on Day 120. However, even though the dose was lowered, the morbidity and mortality continued in this group. Consequently, the animals in this group were euthanized on Day 191 (Week 28).
L1399: The sponsor concluded that no significant differences in the survival rate were noted in males or females at 3 or 8 mg/kg/day when compared to the control group. The survival rate for males and females at 20 mg/kg/day was notably less than the survival rate in the control groups, although the difference was not statistically significant. Decreased survival in the males and females at 20 mg/kg/day was considered BCX7353-related.
L1400: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1401: Tumor incidence data were analyzed using both survival adjusted and unadjusted tests. The unadjusted tests were based on the incidence and number of sites examined for each tumor type. The Cochran-Armitage trend test was calculated and Fisher's exact test14 was used to compare each treatment group with the control group. The survival adjusted test was conducted according to the prevalence/mortality methods described by Peto et al. Evaluation criteria (pvalues of significance) were applied differently for rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) and common tumors (background rate greater than 1%)
L1402: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor concluded that the hemangiosarcoma, malignant, Multicentric neoplasm was significant in both the trend test and the pairwise comparison between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for male rats and the Sarcoma, endometrial stromal, malignant in Uterus with cervix was significant in both the trend test and the pairwise comparison between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for female rats. The Sarcoma, undifferentiated, malignant in Skin/skin, subcutis was significant in the pairwise comparison between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for male rats.
L1403: ## 2.2. Reviewer's analyses
L1404: To verify sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically on December 3, 2019, via NDA214094/0000.
L1405: ## 2.2.1. Survival analysis
L1406: The survival distributions of animals in all five groups were estimated by the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship and homogeneity of survival distributions were tested for the vehicle controls, low, medium and high dose groups using the Likelihood Ratio test and the Log-Rank test. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 7 and 8 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rate are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 9, and 10 in the appendix for males and females, respectively.
L1407: Reviewer's findings: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of survival were 43 (71.67%), 39 (65%), 42 (70%), and 33 (55%) in vehicle control, 3 mg/kg/day, 8 mg/kg/day and 20 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in males and 35 (58.33%), 36 (60%), 37 (61.67%), and 28 (46.67%) in vehicle control, 3 mg/kg/day, 8 mg/kg/day and 20 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in females.
L1408: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups in male or female rats. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and each of the treated groups in male or female rats.
L1409: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L1410: The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{\max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{\max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used. The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons are listed in Tables 11 and 12 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1411: Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.005 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisons for the chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study.
L1412: It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008) showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for Poly-K tests.
L1413: Reviewer's findings: The tumor types in Tables 2 and 3 below showed p-values less than or equal to 0.05 in the trend tests and the tests for pairwise comparisons between the vehicle and the treated groups for male and female rats, respectively. Based on the above criterion for multiple testing adjustment, we make the following statistical conclusions:
L1414: - For male rats, the tumor data analysis showed a statistically significant positive dose response relationship in incidences of the Hemangiosarcoma, Multicentric (P-value=0.0018&lt;0.005), Hemangiosarcoma in Lymph Node, Mesenteric (P-value=0.0001&lt;0.005) and combined tumors of Hemangiosarcoma and Hemangioma in whole body (P-value=0.0044&lt;0.005). The pairwise comparison of the Hemangiosarcoma in Lymph Node, Mesenteric (P-value=0.0049&lt;0.01) between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for male rats is significant at 0.01 level of significance. The pairwise comparison of the Hemangiosarcoma, Multicentric (p-value=0.0166) and between the vehicle control group and the 20 mg/kg/day group for male rats is marginally significant at 0.01 level of significance. However, this tumor type for male rats is definitely significant at 0.05 level of significance in the joint test used by the ECAC but with the more appropriate levels of significance recommended in Lin and Rahman (2018, 2019).
- For female rats, the tumor data analysis showed a statistically significant positive
L1415: dose response relationship in incidence of the Sarcoma, endometrial stromal, Uterus with cervix (P-value=0.0226&lt;0.025).

L1462: ## Tumor analysis:
L1463: - For male mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed a statistically significant increase in incidence of Lymphoma, Hemolymphoreticula (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001), Papilloma, Skin (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001), Papilloma, Stomach (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001) and Squamous cell carcinoma, stomach (P\text{-value}=0.0073&lt;0.05)
- For female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed statistically significant increases in incidence of Lymphoma, Hemolymphoreticula (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001), Papilloma, Skin (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001), Papilloma, Stomach (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001) and Combined tumor of Papilloma and Carcinoma, Skin (P\text{-value}&lt;0.001)

L1548: ## 1 Executive Summary
L1549: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1550: This nonclinical review evaluates the carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat (BCX7353) in animal studies. Berotralstat is a kallikrein inhibitor being developed as a therapy for the prevention of hereditary angioedema (HAE) attacks in adult and adolescent patients. The carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a 2-year traditional bioassay in rats and a 26-week alternative animal model in mice (Reports 806-161 and 20088905, respectively). Results showed that berotralstat was carcinogenic in rats, but not in mice.
L1551: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L1552: The carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a 2-year traditional bioassay in Wistar rats and a 26-week study in B6F1 Tg.rasH2 mice. Rats (60/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 3, 8, and 20-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 104 weeks. Mice (25/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 8, 20, and 50-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 26 weeks. The male rats showed evidence of tumorigenicity as discussed below. No evidence of tumorigenicity was found in mice or female rats.
L1553: The male rats showed statistically significant, dose-related increases in the incidence of hemangiosarcomas (HS) in the mesenteric lymph nodes (Table 1). In pairwise comparisons between the high dose (HD) and controls (C), only the increase in the HS incidence in the mesenteric lymph nodes reached statistical significance (p &lt; 0.01); test for HS in the whole body reached a marginal significance (p=0.017); test for the HS and hemangioma in combination in the whole body did not reach statistical significance (p=0.041). The trend analyses for all the above tumor findings were significant (p &lt; 0.005).
L1554: Table 1: Incidence of Hemangiosarcoma in Male Rats
L1555: Tumor, organ	Sex	Incidence (n = 60)	p value
0	3	8	20	Pairwisea	Trend
Hemangiosarcoma (HS), total	M	2	2	4	9 (15.0%)	0.0166	0.002
Mesenteric lymph node	M	1	1	2	9 (15.0%)	0.0049	0.0001
Skin/adipose tissues	M	1	1	2	0	-b	-
HS + hemangioma, total	M	3	2	4	9 (15.0%)	0.041	0.004
L1556: a. Pairwise comparison between HD and C groups.
L1557: b. (-), not calculated.
L1558: It is a common practice in the evaluation of tumorigenesis to conduct statistical analysis of the HS and hemangioma in the whole body because these tumors are generally present in multiple organs and have similar mechanism of tumorigenesis. Berotralstat induced HS specifically in the mesenteric lymph node in males. The organ and tumor specificity of berotralstat may render it more appropriate to conduct a tissue specific analysis of tumorigenesis.
L1559: The mechanism of tumorigenesis for berotralstat remains unclear. The applicant claims that the mechanism for berotralstat-induced tumorigenesis in rats is not relevant for humans. However, the Division doesn't agree with this claim at this point based on the available data.
L1560: The Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (Exec. CAC) of the Center evaluated the above findings in a meeting held on March 10, 2020 [see meeting minutes, DARRTS ID# 2576054]. The Committee concluded the following:
L1561: - Both rat and mouse studies were adequate to evaluate the carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat.
- Berotralstat tested positive for evidence of carcinogenicity in male rats. The 20-mg/kg/day berotralstat dose for 2-years caused statistically significant increases in the incidence of hemangiosarcomas in the mesenteric lymph node in males. No increases in the incidence of this tumor were observed at \le 8 mg/kg/day. Relevance of the tumorigenicity data in rats to humans is unknown.
- Berotralstat tested negative for tumorigenicity in both sexes in mice and females in rats.

L1576: ## 2.7 Regulatory Background
L1577: BioCryst Pharmaceuticals filed NDA 214094 seeking market registration of berotralstat for the prevention of HAE in adult and adolescent patients on December 3, 2019. The development program of the application was completed in IND 135038.
L1578: The carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a 2-year traditional bioassay in rats and a 26-week study in B6F1 Tg.RasH2 mice (Reports #806-161 and #20088905, respectively). The Agency concurred with the dose selection of the protocol of the 26-week study in transgenic mice on December 14, 2017. The Applicant did not seek the Agency's input on the dose selection of the rat study protocol.

L1584: ## 4 Pharmacology
L1585: Berotralstat is a small molecule kallikrein inhibitor. Kallikrein is a plasma enzyme responsible for the production of bradykinin, a vasodilatory agent. Kallikrein cleaves and releases bradykinin from high-molecular-weight kenenogen. Bradykinin binds to the
L1586: bradykinin B2 receptor and results in dilation of blood vessel, fluid leakage, and edema. Berotralstat inhibited purified plasma human kallikrein (IC_{50} = 5.83 nM).
L1587: ## 5 Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics
L1588: ## 5.1 PK/ADME
L1589: Berotralstat was quickly absorbed after oral administration in animals. Plasma drug levels increased generally in proportion to the administered doses. There were no significant differences in plasma drug levels between sexes. The half-life was 18.5 and 22.7 hr in rats and monkeys, respectively. The oral bioavailability was 33% and 45% in rats and monkeys, respectively. The protein binding capacity was high in humans, rats, and mice (98.7-99.8%) but lower in monkeys and rabbits (74.1-81.9%). The tissues showing the highest maximum concentrations of radioactivity included liver, kidney medulla, spleen, adrenal gland(s), kidney(s), lungs, and pituitary gland. Metabolism of berotralstat was low across species. About 61% of orally administered berotralstat was excreted as unchanged in rats. Less than 10% of berotralstat was metabolized when it was incubated with human cryopreserved hepatocytes for 360 minutes.
L1590: ## 5.2 Toxicokinetics
L1591: Bioaccumulation of BCX7353 occurred in rats, but not in monkeys. Table 2 presents plasma drug AUC levels in rats and monkeys in chronic toxicity studies. In rats, berotralstat AUCs in week 26 were approximately 3 times the AUCs at week 1. The monkeys showed no apparent difference in AUCs between Weeks 1 and 39.

L1595: ## 7 Genetic Toxicity
L1596: Berotralstat tested negative in the Ames test, a chromosomal aberration assay in human peripheral blood lymphocytes in vitro, and an in vivo micronucleus assay in rats.
L1597: ## 8 Carcinogenicity
L1598: ## 8.1 Two-Year Rat Study (806-161)
L1599: ## **Study title:** BCX7353: A 104-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Rats
L1600: Study no.:	806-161
Study report location:	4.2.3.4.1
Conducting laboratory and location:	(b) (4)
Date of study initiation:	September 1, 2016
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	BCX7353.2HCL, 0000007963, 99.1%
CAC concurrence:	No
L1601: ## Key Study Findings
L1602: - Wistar rats dosed by oral gavage with 0 (deionized water), 3, 8, or 20-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 104 weeks showed dose-related increases in the incidence of the following malignant tumors: hemangiosarcoma (HS) in the mesenteric lymph nodes in males and females, undifferentiated sarcoma in the skin in males, and endometrial stromal sarcoma in the uterus in females.
- Only the increase in the incidence of HS in the mesenteric lymph node in HD males reached statistical significance in both the trend test (p &lt; 0.005) and pairwise analyses (p &lt; 0.01).
- Pairwise analysis for HS in HD males (whole body) was considered marginally significant (p=0.0166), although the trend analysis was significant (p &lt; 0.005).
- Pairwise comparison for HS and hemangioma in combination in the whole body was not statistically significant (p=0.041), although the trend analysis was significant (p &lt; 0.005).
- Pairwise analysis for neither undifferentiated sarcoma in the skin in males nor endometrial stromal sarcoma in the uterus in females was statistically significant.
- The HD group showed decreases in mean body weights (by approximately 17.6% and 13.5% in males and females, respectively). These rats also showed audible breathing in the clinical observations. The audible breathing was apparently due to presence of excrete (exudate) in the nasal passage.
- See Table 6 below for the incidence of the above tumors.
L1603: ## Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study
L1604: This study is considered adequate. See minutes of the March 10, 2020 Exec CAC meeting (DARRTS ID 4576064). The duration of treatment (i.e., 104 weeks) is adequate. The dose selection seems to be appropriate. The high dose of 20 mg/kg/day was one-third to half of the lethal dose of 40 – 60 mg/kg/day. [see Footnote 1 on page 10]. Dose-related responses in both neoplastic and non-neoplastic findings were observed.
L1605: Neoplastic responses were discussed above in the Key Study Finding section. Non-neoplastic findings are listed in Table 7. Briefly, treatment-related lesions were observed in several organs and tissues in the HD group: adrenal glands, kidney, lung, lymph nodes, skeletal muscle, intestine, and spleen. The lesions were mostly cell vacuolations.
L1606: The Agency did not concur with the dose selection of berotralstat for the 2-year rat study because the applicant did not seek the Agency's input on the dose selection. However, the study results as discussed in the review indicated that the dose-selection was appropriate.
L1607: ## Appropriateness of Test Model
L1608: The model is appropriate. This strain of rats was used in the repeat-dose toxicity studies of berotralstat. The rats showed dose and temporal effects of berotralstat in oral studies up to 26 weeks in treatment duration.
L1609: ## Evaluation of Tumor Findings
L1610: Berotralstat is an epigenetic carcinogen in rats. Male rats dosed with 20-mg/kg/day berotralstat (HD dose) orally for 2 years showed statistically significant increases in the incidence of HS in the mesenteric lymph node (Tables 6 and 7). Hemangiosarcoma is a common malignant tumor in rats.
L1611: Dr. Zhuang Miao conducted the statistical analysis of the data. A draft review was provided to the DPARP nonclinical team on February 14, 2020. Dr. Miao conducted the following two tests in the review: trend test and pairwise comparison. The review uses the following criteria below to define a positive result:
L1612: - Trend test alone: 0.005 and 0.025 for common and rare tumors, respectively
- Pairwise comparison alone: 0.01 and 0.05 for common and rare tumors, respectively.
- Joint tests (trend + pairwise): respective p values for common and rare tumors are 0.005 and 0.025 in trend test and 0.05 and 0.1 in pairwise comparisons
L1613: HS is considered a common tumor in rats. Only HS in the mesenteric lymph node in high-dose male rats met all 3 criteria above and was considered positive.
L1614: Pairwise comparison for the incidence of HS between the HD and C groups in whole body was marginally significant (p=0.017). Pairwise comparison for the incidence of HS and hemangioma in combination between the HD and Control groups was insignificant (p=0.041), although trend analysis showed positive dose responses (p &lt; 0.005).
L1615: There were also dose-related increases in the incidence of HS in HD females, undifferentiated sarcoma in the skin in males, and endometrial stromal sarcoma in the uterus in females. None of these increases reached statistical significance in pairwise comparison. Skin sarcoma and endometrial stromal sarcoma were considered rare tumors for this analysis.
L1616: The HD group showed decreases in mean body weight, audible breathing, and presence of excrete (exudate) in the nasal passage. It is unclear whether these observations had any major impact on the tumorigenicity of berotralstat in rats.
L1617: ## Methods
L1618: Doses:	0, 3, 8, and 20 mg/kg/day (Table 3)
The above dose schedule excluded another group - extra high dose (EH) group that was terminated prematurely at Week 28 (day 191).1
Frequency of dosing:	Daily
Dose volume:	2.5 mL/kg
Route of administration:	Oral gavage
Formulation/Vehicle:	Aqueous solution/ deionized water
Basis of dose selection:	Mortality, the HD was 1/3 to half of the lethal dose
Species/Strain:	Rat, Wistar Han [Crl:WI(Han)]
Number/Sex/Group:	60
Age:	7.5 weeks on day 1 of dosing
Animal housing:	paired
Paradigm for dietary restriction:	No dietary restriction
Dual control employed:	No
Interim sacrifice:	No
Satellite groups:	5 and 12/sex/dose in the control and BCX7353 treatment groups for TK analysis. The TK animals were sacrificed after blood sampling on day 182.
Deviation from study protocol:	Not remarkable
L1619: 1 The review excludes the extra-high dose (EH, Group 5) group from the evaluation and discussions of the carcinogenicity results of BCX7353. This is to enhance the clarity of the discussion and evaluation of the study results. The EH group primarily provides information that supports the dose selection of the carcinogenicity study design. This group was terminated for humane reasons prematurely in Week 28 (Day 191). By the time of termination of the animals, the group mean body weights was decreased by 16.2% and 8.7% in males and females, respectively. Five of the females died. The dosing regimen was 60 and 40 mg/kg/day for weeks 1 – 17 and 18 – 27, respectively. No data relevant to tumorigenicity was obtained from any of the animals.
L1620: Animals in this EH group generally showed clinical observations similar to that of the HD group. These included decreases in body weight and food consumption, salivation, thin appearance and audible breathing. Microscopic changes, however, were unremarkable because the findings were generally less severe and with lower incidence.

L1623: ## OBSERVATIONS AND RESULTS
L1624: ## Mortality
L1625: The HD group in males and females showed higher incidence of unscheduled deaths (Table 4). The respective number of deaths in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups was 17, 21, 18, and 26 in males, and 25, 24, 23, and 32 in the females. The respective survival rate in the HD group and remaining groups was 55% and 65 – 70% in males, and 47% and 58 – 62% in females. In short, the HD group showed mortality rate at least 10 – 12% higher than other groups. None of the deaths were considered HS-related.

L1652: There was no apparent sex difference in the HS incidence between these early deaths/sacrifice. Two (of 9) males either died or were sacrificed (Rats #4007 and #4024 on days 714 and 670, respectively). Three of the 7 females with HS in the lymph nodes

The report identified these tumor as “mortality-independent” primary tumors. None of these rats had HS. The statistical analysis showed p values of 0.045 for trend test and 0.107 for pairwise comparison between the HD and control. This tumor type did not meet any criterion for a positive result.
L1655: **Uterine tumors:** Two MD and three HD females showed endometrial stromal sarcoma. The tumor was the cause of death in 4 of the 5 rats (2 in both MD and HD). The 2 MD rats (#3522 and #3548) were sacrificed on Days 601 and 606, respectively. The 2 HD rats (#4511 and #4520) were sacrificed on days 722 and 713, respectively. The remaining HD rat (#4528) was sacrificed as scheduled. This tumor type did not meet any criterion for a positive result.

L1657: Table 8 summarizes notable non-neoplastic findings. Treatment-related lesions were observed in the following organs and tissues: adrenal glands, kidney, lung, lymph nodes, skeletal muscle, intestine, and spleen. The most common finding is vacuolated or foamy macrophages (adrenals, lymph nodes, intestine, and spleen) and is observed mostly in the HD group. Other findings included accumulation of alveolar macrophages and exudate in the nasal cavity in both sexes and myofiber degeneration of the skeletal muscles in males.

L1666: ## 8.2 Transgenic Mouse Study (20088905)
L1667: ## Study title: BCX7353: A 26-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Tg.rasH2 mice
L1668: Study no.: 20088905
Study report location: 4.2.3.4.1
Conducting laboratory and location: [REDACTED] (b) (4)
Date of study initiation: June 14, 2018
GLP compliance: Yes
QA statement: Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity: BCX7353.2HCL, 0000038590, 99.8%
CAC concurrence: Yes
L1669: ## Key Study Findings
L1670: B6F1 Tg.RasH2 mice dosed orally with ≤ 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 weeks did not show any evidence of carcinogenicity. Mice were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 8, 20, and 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 weeks. There were no significant dose-related increases in the incidence of any tumors.
L1671: ## **Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study**
L1672: This study was adequate. The study design was routine for transgenic models. The treatment duration was 26 weeks. There were 25 mice/sex/dose. The Agency concurred with the dose selection. See nonclinical review completed by Dr. Dong Zhao on December 14, 2017 (DARRTS ID 4195500).
L1673: ## **Appropriateness of Test Models**
L1674: The model is appropriate. The Executive CAC reviewed the model and dose selection in the December 14, 2017 meeting. See nonclinical review completed by Dr. Dong Zhao on December 14, 2017 (DARRTS ID 4195500).
L1675: ## **Evaluation of Tumor Findings**
L1676: B6F1 Tg.RasH2 mice dosed by oral gavage with up to 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 weeks showed no dose-related increases in the incidence of any tumors. A positive control (75 mg/kg/day N-nitroso-N-methylurea, or NMU) group showed increases in the incidence of tumors typical of the treatment in this model. Berotralstat was considered negative for carcinogenicity in the Tg.RasH2 mouse model.
L1677: The HD male group treated with berotralstat showed a higher incidence of HS in the spleen (2/25), but the C and HD females showed similar incidences (3/25 and 2/25, respectively). The finding is not considered significant or treatment-related.
L1678: Many tissues in 6 HD males were not examined because tissues were missing. The missing tissues included pituitary, thymus, thyroid/parathyroid, adrenals, lungs, trachea, gallbladder, urinary bladder, rectum, mesenteric lymph nodes, prostate, epididymides, and larynx. See Protocol deviation. The lack of tumor information in these organs would not probably affect the validity of the study results and the overall interpretation of study findings.
L1679: ## **Methods**
L1680: Doses:	0, 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day, see Table 10.
Frequency of dosing:	Daily
Dose volume:	2.5 mL/kg
Route of administration:	Oral gavage

L1687: ## Mortality
L1688: Not remarkable. There were no significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control and the berotralstat treatment groups. The number of mice that survived to the terminal sacrifice was 22 - 25 out of 25 in the control and berotralstat treatment groups, respectively, Table 11. Four animals in the berotralstat treatment groups (1-HD M, 2 MD Fs, and 1 HD F) died of gavage accidents. Berotralstat treatment groups showed no apparent increases in tumor-related deaths.

L1711: ## Non-Neoplastic
L1712: Non-neoplastic findings were noticed in the nasal cavity, eye, liver, lymph nodes, and spleen (Table 15). The findings in the nose included inflammation in all berotralstat treatment groups in both sexes and erosion/ulceration of respiratory epithelium in the HD males. Findings in the eyes (dysplasia), liver (inflammation and hepatocellular atrophy), lymph nodes (decreases in cellularity) and spleen (decreases in cellularity) were minimal in severity, low in incidence or occurred in only one sex. They also lacked significant dose-response relationship.

L1718: ## Toxicokinetics
L1719: Toxicokinetics of berotralstat was studied in satellite groups in non-transgenic mice. Blood samples were collected at hours 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 post dosing on days 1 and 182 (n = 3 mice/time point). Table 16 presents AUC and Cmax data. Both mean AUC and Cmax were dose-proportional in week 1 but supra proportional to dose in week 26. It is unknown whether the data reflect the plasma drug levels in the transgenic mice.

L1723: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L1724: The carcinogenic potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a traditional 2-yr bioassay in rats and a 26-week alternative assay in a transgenic mouse model. The respective berotralstat dose in the LD, MD, and HD groups was 3, 8, and 20 mg/kg/day in rats and 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day in mice. There were 60 and 25 animals/sex/dose in rats and Tg.rasH2 transgenic mice, respectively. The results showed that berotralstat was carcinogenic in male rats, but not in female rats, nor in transgenic mouse. Relevance of the tumorigenicity data in rats to humans is unknown.
L1725: The carcinogenicity of berotralstat in male rats was demonstrated by dose-related increases in the incidence of HS in the mesenteric lymph node (incidence: 1, 1, 2, and 9 in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups, respectively). Only the increase in the HS incidence in the HD group reached statistical significance (p &lt; 0.01 in pairwise comparison). The increase in the HS incidence in the whole body in the HD group reached statistically marginal significance in pairwise comparison (p = 0.017). Both were positive in the trend analysis (p &lt; 0.005).
L1726: The Applicant considers berotralstat an epigenetic carcinogen in rats. The review agrees with the Applicant. The review, however, disagrees with the Applicant about the relevance of the tumorigenic findings to humans. The Exec. CAC agreed with the review. See minutes of the Exec. CAC meeting dated March 10, 2020 (DARRTS ID# 4576054).
L1727: The applicant contends that the HS finding is irrelevant to humans and of no safety concern for the proposed clinical use of the drug. Section 13.1 of the proposed labeling states “[t]his finding is considered rodent specific”. The Applicant argues that their conclusion was based on the mechanism of tumorigenesis of HS in rodents. Specifically, HS in rats is secondary to hypoxia in rats, which will not happen in humans receiving berotralstat treatment. There is evidence for and against this argument.
L1728: Primary evidence in support of the hypoxia hypothesis is a paper by Cohen et al. published in 2009 [*Toxicol Sci*, 2009;111(1)4-18]. This is a review article (referred to as the Cohen paper hereafter) discussing the mechanism of HS formation associated with non-genotoxic compounds in rodents and the relevance of the HS in rodents to humans. Both genotoxic and non-genotoxic compounds can induce HS in rodents (mostly in mice). The mechanism of carcinogenesis of HS differs between genotoxic and non-genotoxic compounds. Non-genotoxic compounds can produce HS in mice secondary to tissue hypoxia. Such compounds include pesticides, industrial chemicals, and pharmaceuticals. Pharmaceuticals include “calcium channel blockers, antipsychotics, PDE-5 inhibitors, DPP-4 inhibitors, antiarrhythmics, gonadotropin receptor antagonists, antisense compounds, nitric oxide releasers, hemolytic compounds, and vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF) inducers.”
L1729: The Cohen paper concludes that hypoxia and macrophage activation is a common mechanism of action of non-genotoxic drug-induced HS in rodents. The paper states the carcinogenesis appears to “be a convergence of multiple initiating events (e.g., hemolysis, decreased respiration, adipocyte growth) leading to either dysregulated angiogenesis and/or erythropoiesis that results from hypoxia and macrophage activation. These events lead to the release of angiogenic growth factors and cytokines that stimulate endothelial cell proliferation, which, if sustained, provide the milieu that can lead to HS formation.”
L1730: The Cohen paper discussed in depth the hypoxia-related HS induced by two pharmaceuticals: pregabalin and troglitazone. Pregabalin (Lyrica, NDA 21-446) caused HS in the tissues where spontaneous HS are commonly observed in mice: liver, spleen,
L1731: and bone marrow in B6C3F1 and CD1 mice, but not any tissue in rats.2 The paper states: "The precipitating event in pregabalin-associated HS development in mice appears to be decreased respiratory function, including decreased respiration rate and decreased minute volumes, along with alterations in acid-base balance, gradually shifting to a relative alkalotic state." This eventually leads to local tissue hypoxia.
L1732: Troglitazone (Rezulin, withdrawn from the market) produced primarily HS in the skin (subcutaneous adipose tissue) in mice, but not rats. Troglitazone produced local tissue hypoxia in mice "due to the inability of angiogenesis to keep pace with growth of the fat pad. The local tissue hypoxia results in [hypoxia-inducible factor] HIF-1a activation which can activate VEGF secretion and/or macrophage activation".
L1733: The Applicant argues that HS in rats treated with berotralstat was also a result of hypoxia, which occurred because of narrowing of the nasal airway passage and breathing difficulty. Narrowing of nasal airway passage was demonstrated by the presence of exudate in the nasal cavity in berotralstat -treated rats in microscopic examinations. The respective incidence of the presence of luminal exudate in nasal cavity in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups (out of 60) was 2, 2, 8, and 23 in males and 1, 1, 4, 25 in females. Breathing difficulty was demonstrated by audible breathing. The respective incidence of audible breathing in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups (observations/animals) was 5/4, 1/1, 1/1, and 1160/47 in males and 3/3, 3/3, 2/2, and 470/45 in females.
L1734: The review agrees with the Applicant that the HD rats showed breathing difficulty, which suggest the presence of hypoxia. Rodents are obligated nose-breathing animals. Presence of exudate in the nasal passage may impede the airflow and causing breathing difficulty which is indicated by the observation of audible breathing. The difficulty of breathing may not indicate necessarily tissue hypoxia in rats.
L1735: There are also factors arguing against applying the hypoxia hypothesis to the interpretation of the berotralstat study results in rats. This includes differences in animal species, tumor locations, and drug classes. Table 17 presents pharmaceuticals that induce HS in the Cohen paper. None of these drugs induced HS in rats. Regarding to tumor locations, the Cohen paper reported that HS "most frequently occur in liver, spleen, heart, or subcutaneous adipose tissue", which is consistent with the findings observed with these drugs. Further, mice have higher spontaneous incidence of HS than rats or humans because they have lower antioxidant levels (e.g., Vitamin E) than rats and humans. The high spontaneous background rate may contribute to their enhanced susceptibility to developing HS.
L1736: However, berotralstat does not belong to any of the HS-inducing drug classes. The rat has low spontaneous HS rates. Further, rats and mice differ in organs/tissues with HS. Hypoxia could have been present in rats treated with high dose of berotralstat, but the
L1737: 2 Section 5 of the Label of pregabalin states: "In standard preclinical *in vivo* lifetime carcinogenicity studies of LYRICA, an unexpectedly high incidence of hemangiosarcoma was identified in two different strains of mice [see *Nonclinical Toxicology* (13.1)]. The clinical significance of this finding is unknown."
L1738: hypoxia hypothesis alone cannot fully explain the mechanism of HS formation in rats. The lymph node specificity of HS in rats could be related to the high local concentration of the parent compound and metabolites, alone or in combination.

L1742: There is insufficient evidence to support the Applicant's argument that HS in the mesenteric lymph node associated with berotralstat treatment in male rats is a species-specific finding. Thus, relevance of the carcinogenic finding in rats to humans is unknown at present time.

L1750: The Committee did not address the sponsor's proposed statistical evaluation for the 2-yr carcinogenicity bioassays, as this does not affect the sponsor's ability to initiate the bioassays. The sponsor may seek guidance on the statistical evaluation of bioassay results from agency staff separately. Data files should be submitted electronically following the CDER/CBER Guidance for Industry, Providing Regulatory Submissions in Electronic Format - Standardized Study Data (December 2014) and the latest Study Data Technical Conformance Guide.
L1751: IND# 135058
L1752: Drug Name: BCX7353
L1753: Sponsor: Biocryst Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L1754: ## Background
L1755: Biocryst Pharmaceuticals is developing BCX7353, a small molecule kallikrein inhibitor, as an oral treatment of hereditary angioedema (HAE) under IND 135058. Kallikrein is a plasma enzyme that regulates the production of bradykinin. Bradykinin, as a vasodilatory agent, binds to the bradykinin B2 receptor and results in dilation of blood vessel, fluid leakage, and edema. BCX7353 was negative in the *in vitro* bacterial mutagenicity (Ames test), *in vitro* mammalian cell mutagenicity (human peripheral blood lymphocytes mutation assay) studies, and *in vivo* rat bone marrow micronucleus study. In the present submission, the sponsor submitted a Special Protocol Assessment request (SD 13 received 11/14/2017) for a carcinogenicity study evaluating BCX7353 in RasH2 transgenic (Tg.RasH2) mice.
L1756: ## Tg.rasH2 Mouse Carcinogenicity Study Dose Selection
L1757: BCX7353 dose selection is supported by the results of a 4-week toxicity study in Tg.RasH2 wildtype littermate mice (Study 20088903) that evaluated oral gavage doses of 0 (vehicle of water), 25, 50 or 125 mg/kg/day. The maximal tolerable dose (MTD) has been identified as 50 mg/kg/day based on mortalities, clinical signs and body weight decreases at 125 mg/kg/day and slight decreases in body weight gain at 50 mg/kg/day.
L1758: Biocryst is proposing dose levels of 0, 8, 20 and 60 mg/kg/day for the 6-month carcinogenicity study in Tg.RasH2 hemizygous mice. The sponsor's proposed high-dose was set at one-half the lethal dose of 125 mg/kg/day.
L1759: ## Executive CAC Recommendations and Conclusions
L1760: ## Tg.rasH2 Mouse
L1761: - The Committee recommended doses of 0, 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day for males and females with the high dose based on providing approximately one-third the exposure of the lethal dose of 125 mg/kg in the 28-day mouse toxicology study.
- The mid- and low-doses for both sexes were selected based on adequate spacing of AUC.
- The Committee noted that hematology at the end of the study is not needed for FDA.
- The Committee noted that the use of toxicokinetic dose groups is not needed for the FDA.
- The sponsor should conduct histopathological examination in all dose groups.
L1762: Karen Davis Bruno, PhD
Chair, Executive CAC

L1775: The following information reflects a brief summary of the Committee discussion and its recommendations.
L1776: **Application Type and Number(s):** NDA 214094
L1777: **Drug Name:** Berotralstat (BCX7353)
L1778: **Sponsor:** BioCryst Pharmaceuticals
L1779: ## Background
L1780: BioCryst Pharmaceuticals filed NDA 214094 on December 3, 2019 seeking marketing approval of berotralstat, a kallikrein inhibitor, for the prevention of hereditary angioedema in adult and adolescent patients. The carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a 2-year traditional bioassay in rats and in a 26-week study in transgenic mice (Reports #806-161 and #20088905, respectively). The Agency concurred with the dose selection of the protocol of the 26-week study in transgenic mice on December 14, 2017.
L1781: ## Mouse Carcinogenicity Study
L1782: B6F1 Tg.rasH2 mice (25/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 8, 20, and 50-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 26 weeks. No evidence of tumorigenicity was found in either sex. A positive control (75 mg/kg/day N-nitroso-N-methylurea) group showed increases in the incidence of tumors typical of the treatment in this model.
L1783: ## Rat Carcinogenicity Study
L1784: Rats (60/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 3, 8, and 20-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 104 weeks. The male rats showed statistically significant, dose-related increases in the incidence of hemangiosarcomas (HS) in the mesenteric lymph nodes (Table 1). In pairwise comparisons between the high dose (HD) and controls (C), only the increase in the HS incidence in the mesenteric lymph nodes reached statistical significance (p &lt; 0.01); the test for HS in the whole body reached a marginal significance (p=0.017); the test for the HS and hemangioma in combination in the whole body did not reach statistical significance (p=0.041). The trend analyses for all the above tumor findings were significant (p &lt; 0.005).
L1785: The high dose rats in the study showed clinical signs of labored and audible breathing during the study. Microscopic examination revealed presence of nasal exudate and sinus histiocytosis in the mesenteric lymph node. These findings suggest potential presence of tissue hypoxia in these rats.
L1786: The relevance of the HS findings associated with berotralstat treatment in rats to human is unknown. Cohen et al [Toxicol Sci, 2009;111(1)4-18] reported that HS and hemangiomas associated with non-genotoxic chemicals in rodents may be attributed to hypoxia. Hypoxia that appeared present in the high dose in rats may play a significant role in the carcinogenesis of berotralstat, but tissue specificity of the finding suggests that other factors may also play important roles. These factors may include higher exposures of the tissue to the parent compound, metabolites, and/or drug impurities. Overall, relevance of the HS finding to humans remains unknown.
L1787: **Table 18: Incidence of Hemangiosarcoma in Male Rats**

L1791: ## Executive CAC Conclusions
L1792: ## Mouse study:
L1793: - The Committee concluded the carcinogenicity study is adequate and negative for drug-related neoplasms in males and females.
L1794: ## Rat study:
L1795: - The Committee concluded the carcinogenicity study is adequate.
- The Committee concluded that berotralstat is positive for drug-related neoplasms in male rats. The high dose of berotralstat (20 mg/kg/day) caused a statistically significant increase in hemangiosarcoma in male rats in the mesenteric lymph node. ExecCAC criteria for statistical significance were not reached for hemangiosarcoma in all tissues or for hemangiosarcoma and hemangioma combined in all tissues.
- The Committee concluded that the mechanism of carcinogenesis of berotralstat in rats remains unclear and that the relevance of berotralstat-induced tumorigenesis in rats to humans is unknown.
- The Committee concluded that berotralstat tested negative for drug-related neoplasms in female rats.

Chair, Executive CAC

Group 3: Placebo QD \times 24 weeks

Group 2: 150 mg BCX7353 QD \times 24 weeks

L1817: Scatter plot showing the correlation of Plasma Berotralstat Concentrations (BCX7353 Concentration in ng/mL) on the X-axis (0 to 250) and Ex-vivo Kallikrein Inhibition (%) on the Y-axis (-100 to 100). The data points show a sigmoidal curve, indicating that inhibition increases rapidly up to about 50 ng/mL and then plateaus around 90% inhibition. A fitted sigmoidal maximum-efficacy model is overlaid on the data.
L1818: Scatter plot showing Investigator-Confirmed Attack Rate (attacks per week) versus Berotralstat Plasma AUC_tau (hr*ng/mL). The X-axis ranges from 0 to 10000 hr*ng/mL. The Y-axis ranges from 0.0 to 2.5 attacks per week. Data points are colored by dose group (55 mg, 110 mg, 218 mg, 300 mg). A dashed regression line is shown, along with 95% confidence intervals (dashed lines). The Pearson correlation coefficient is -0.1037 (P-value = 0.4690) for N = 51.

The plot displays data points (green dots and blue dots) and a solid regression line, along with dashed lines representing the 95% Confidence Interval (CI) for the mean predicted values. The regression line shows a negative correlation.

L1824: Figure 1 displays two Kaplan-Meier survival curves. The left plot, labeled 'Male', shows Product-Limit Survival Estimates for male rats across four dose groups (0, 3, 8, and 20 mg/kg/day) and a Vehicle Control group. The right plot, labeled 'Female', shows Product-Limit Survival Estimates for female rats across the same four dose groups and Vehicle Control group. Both plots show Survival Probability on the Y-axis (0.0 to 1.0) versus Time in week of death or sacrifice on the X-axis (0 to 100). The HD group (20 mg/kg/day) shows the lowest survival probability in both males and females, indicating higher mortality.
L1825: Figure 2 displays two line graphs showing the time course of mean body weights in rats over 97 weeks. The left graph is for Male Rats, and the right graph is for Female Rats. Both graphs plot Body Weight (g) on the Y-axis against Time (Weeks) on the X-axis. Four treatment groups are compared: Vehicle Control (red), 3 mg/kg/day (orange), 8 mg/kg/day (purple), and 20 mg/kg/day (blue). In Male Rats, the 20 mg/kg/day group shows the largest decrease in body weight compared to the control groups, reaching approximately 600 g at 97 weeks. In Female Rats, the 20 mg/kg/day group also shows a significant decrease, reaching approximately 350 g at 97 weeks, while the control groups remain around 370 g.
